frontend/docs/architecture\production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md:29:`foundation_revision` holds the public Site definition, not simulated world inputs. `signal_binding_revision` is both the server's registered gateway mapping and the semantic role binding; gateway downloads an immutable versioned snapshot with a canonical content hash over an authenticated channel. A mapping set must reference an effective Foundation revision and every bound asset/channel must exist at publication. The raw envelope still states its mapping set/version for audit. A batch is homogeneous in mapping and Foundation reference; the gateway closes one batch and opens another when either changes. Ingestion checks that the **referenced registered versions' original valid ranges** cover each envelope's source time, even if a later configuration correction superseded them while the gateway was offline. It records the accepted-as basis and flags an outdated mapping/configuration; it rejects unregistered versions or times outside the referenced range. Analytics at a later `knowledge_at` may rebind and renormalize the immutable original reading under a corrected time-valid version, recording those new revision IDs in its input set. This never edits accepted evidence. A record dated before any configured period is rejected with a specific reason; it is not assigned current configuration.

frontend/docs/architecture\production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md:173:`QueryScope.window` is half-open `[start,end)` and required for analytic/product queries. `knowledge_at` makes historical audits reproducible; normal UI defaults to current server time. `selected_revision_id` freezes a drill-down even if the surrounding window changes. `AnalyticInputSet.input_sha256` hashes sorted evidence IDs **and their content digests**, resolved configuration/binding revisions, window, analytic/model version and canonical role coverage. The financial consequence has a separate basis digest including its assumption revisions. Missing required roles produce a bounded Unknown/Indeterminate assessment with explicit reason; the analytic never reads world state or a Lab run ID. `FinancialConsequence` has no amount when the technical quantity is supported but price/demand is not. `Measured`, `Modelled`, `Extrapolated` and `Scenario` are distinct bases; UI must expose them.
frontend/docs/architecture\production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md:179:3. **Decide records.** In one Postgres transaction, lock batch row; for each unprocessed ordinal parse strict envelope, verify identity/sequence and referenced mapping/Foundation validity at `source_time`, normalize with the registered as-accepted conversion, and insert acceptance or rejection plus disposition. Unique message key arbitrates concurrent duplicate deliveries. Equal digest is `DUPLICATE`; changed digest is `CONFLICT` and points to first accepted evidence. Update watermark and create/coalesce recompute jobs in the same commit. Chunk a large batch into short committed transactions; receipt exposes `IN_PROGRESS` until all ordinals are durable. A client retries the same batch/bytes and resumes from the persisted watermark. Return a terminal receipt only after all decisions commit.
frontend/docs/architecture\production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md:180:4. **Assess.** Worker leases dirty site/domain/window jobs, selects accepted evidence by source time plus interval overlap at a declared knowledge cutoff, resolves the exact public configuration, builds `AnalyticInputSet`, and runs the versioned pure analytic. In one commit insert `AnalyticRun`, immutable assessment/finding/consequence revisions when basis changed, update projections and mark job done. Retry of same input hash is a no-op. Late or out-of-order evidence recomputes the affected and neighboring continuity window; it never mutates old revisions. Model deployment schedules explicit re-evaluation of supported windows.
frontend/docs/architecture\production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md:189:**Acceptance checks for the first slices:** power loss after SQLite enqueue and after server commit; duplicate batch with identical bytes; same ID with different bytes; partial batch crash/retry; reordered and late samples; two same-timestamp samples with different sequences; mapping/Foundation version change during offline period; wrong gateway/site credential; one rejected sibling among valid samples; central diagnostic receipt/reload and stale-heartbeat behavior. S2–S7 use simulated input only. At the first real pilot, select one real capture or labelled vendor-format wire fixture and then compare physical and simulated normalized envelopes and downstream claims aside from provenance. Test lineage by opening a visible claim and resolving every evidence and assumption ID, rather than asserting only rendered text.

frontend/docs/architecture\production-shaped-mvp.md:41:Adapters poll or subscribe to Modbus/serial, meters, PLC/BMS and vendor HTTP/OPC-UA interfaces as actually encountered at a pilot; each yields a typed device report with source clock, unit, quality and device identity. The gateway generates `SourceEnvelope` after mapping, with `source_time`, `published_at`, quality/unavailable reason, interval bounds where relevant, gateway/source/device/signal IDs, mapping version and a stable message identity. frontend/docs/architecture\production-shaped-mvp.md:49:Use `POST /api/ingestion/batches` over TLS with gateway credentials scoped to site/source, bounded batch size, rate limits and a queryable batch receipt. Authenticate the declared site and gateway against registered identity; do not trust `source_mode` or mapping/version references merely because they are present in JSON. Resolve the mapping and Foundation version effective **at source time**, including old versions after edits. Validate schema, clocks, units, role binding and provenance, then atomically persist acceptance decision, raw envelope digest/reference, normalized accepted record or rejection/conflict, and batch progress in Postgres. Unique keys for `(site, gateway, source, message_id)` and `(site, batch_id)` make retries idempotent; same key and different bytes is a conflict, not overwrite. Return per-envelope disposition and a receipt only after commit. Do not let a malformed sibling hide valid records. An outbox/work table in the same transaction schedules analytic recomputation; a worker leases jobs, retries safely and publishes revisioned read models. frontend/docs/architecture\production-shaped-mvp.md:55:Keep explicit relational entities: `Tenant/Site`, `Asset` (stable physical identity), `FoundationRevision` (valid-time, recorded-time, topology, objectives, controls), `SignalBindingRevision`, `Gateway/Source`, `IngestionBatch`, `AcceptedEvidence`, `EvidenceRejection`, `AnalyticRun/AssessmentRevision`, `Finding` + immutable `FindingRevision`, `FinancialAssumptionRevision`, `FinancialConsequenceRevision`, `Action/ActionEvent`, `VerificationPlanRevision/Outcome`. frontend/docs/architecture\production-shaped-mvp.md:71:The world owns physics, controls, scenario causes and oracle expectations; simulated devices own only what their real counterparts could report. The simulated gateway cannot read private world state except through device reports. Its SQLite store persists staged envelopes and upload state exactly as a physical gateway would. Preserve Lab Draft/Stage/Commit only as a **release gate**: Draft may execute and inspect private truth; Stage seals public envelopes; Commit authorizes delivery through the same batch API and records a manifest/receipt. Commit cannot write evidence, Findings or site projections. Private truth, controller intent, causes, oracle and run controls live in a separate Lab store and Lab-only API with access controls; no foreign key or import from normal analytics to those objects. Simulated provenance may label a claim, never alter its calculation.
frontend/docs/architecture\production-shaped-mvp.md:75:The shared shell owns identity, site/window selection, evidence drawer, claim status, Findings, money and work navigation. A small registry keyed by `site_type` selects explicit evidence roles, analytic functions, summary sections and labels. A role binding maps physical signals to analytic inputs for a *valid period*; it is not a runtime plugin. Missing roles lower or block claim capability. Mini-grid roles include generator state/power, feeder demand, PV availability, battery reserve/SOC, fuel and service baseline; cold-chain roles include chamber temperature, setpoint/policy, door/defrost, compressor and backup state; EV roles include session request/energy/end state, connector availability, charger limits and tariff. Keep roles minimal per analytic, not one global schema that pretends domains are interchangeable.

# Role Configuration

Roles are project responsibilities. Agent bindings are configurable defaults.

## Default Bindings

| Role | Default agent | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Architect | Codex; scoped Claude exception for the v6.9 site mock-up work on `arch/canonical-v69-pivot` (2026-10-06; see below) | Creates feature maps, causal sequencing, protected seams, and feature-to-task guidance. |
| Planner | Codex (Claude exception ended at the 2026-10-03 21:23 reset; see below) | Converts reviewed feature guidance into small UI-verifiable task definitions. |
| Implementer | Claude | Builds the active task, runs checks, and prepares the review packet. |
| Reviewer | Configurable independent agent; Codex by default (see below) | Reviews work independently from the author. |

## Rules

- Keep role responsibilities stable even if the bound agent changes.
- Do not assume Planner, Architect, or Implementer means a specific vendor unless
  this file says so.
- Implementer may refine implementation steps, but should not redefine feature
  sequencing without Architect or user review.
- Reviewer must be independent from the agent that authored the implementation.
- Claude Code commands in `.claude/commands/` should read this file before
  acting so they can either run the role or prepare a handoff for the configured
  agent.

## Decision Requests Go To The Active Architect

Set by the owner on 2026-10-04 (verbatim in `.agent/owner-T028-closeout-2026-10-04.md`):
"for all decsion requests, let the active architect make them and inform me if
the architect wants me to weigh in as well with its justification and decision".

So the coordinator does not put decision requests to the owner. It sends them
to the Architect bound above, which decides, records the decision with its
justification, and informs the owner. Where the Architect wants the owner to
weigh in, it says so, giving its decision and justification, and the owner may
confirm or overrule. Owner acceptance of a slice (USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED) is not
a decision request and still goes to the owner.

## Architect Was Temporarily Claude, And Is Codex Again

On 2026-09-24 the user set Architect to Claude because Codex had exhausted its
usage limit twice in one day while the task queue waited on an Architect
review. That entry said to revert when capacity allowed. Codex capacity
returned at 18:21 the same day and the binding was reverted at 18:53, so the
exception is closed and the rule below applies again without qualification.

Two Architect passes were produced under the exception, both on
`arch/queue-review-feedback` and both merged: the queue resequencing at
`2970d74` and the hardening-gate removal at `e02d267`. A later reader checking
who authored them should know they were Claude by an explicit, recorded
decision rather than by the coordinator routing around this file.

The lesson worth keeping is the one the exception was written to respect: when
a binding has to change, change it here first and then act. Routing around a
control is worse than the mistake the control prevents.

## Invoking A Codex-Bound Role

A role bound to Codex is run by invoking Codex, not by dispatching the
same-named Claude subagent and merging what it returns. Codex is on PATH and
runs non-interactively:

```
codex exec "<the role brief>"
```

**The obligation is the coordinator's, not the subagent's.** On 2026-09-23 the
Architect correctly flagged three times that it was producing Architect output
while this file bound the role to Codex. Each time the coordinator relayed the
flag to the user as a question and merged the output anyway. A subagent noticing
a binding violation is not a control, because it has already done the work by
the time it can tell anyone. The control is that the coordinator invokes the
bound tool in the first place.

So: before dispatching `assetops-architect` or `assetops-planner`, check the
binding above. If it is Codex, run `codex exec`. The Claude subagents of those
names refuse Codex-bound work rather than producing it.

## The Reviewer Binding, And Its Backup

The Reviewer is **Codex by default**. When Codex is unavailable - usage limit,
outage, or a reset hours away - the backup is a **fresh Claude
`assetops-reviewer` subagent**, and the substitution needs no permission. A
slice waiting a day for quota is worse than a slice reviewed by the weaker of
two independent readers.

**Independence is the rule the backup must not break.** The backup is a NEW
agent instance. It is never the agent that implemented the slice, and never
that agent resumed with a different instruction. An implementer asked to
review its own work will confirm it, which is why the binding exists.

State in the report which reviewer produced it. A Claude review of Claude
implementation is genuinely weaker than a Codex one on the same slice, and a
later reader deciding how much weight to give a verdict needs to know which
they are reading. Where the substitution happened because of quota, say so and
say what was left unjudged, so the question can go back to Codex if it matters
enough to wait for.

## Which Reviewer For Which Round

Set by the user on 2026-09-26 to conserve Codex quota, which has been the
binding constraint on this milestone three times. This is not the same as the
backup rule above: it applies whether or not Codex is available.

**Codex reviews discovery.** The first review of any slice, a contract version
move, a new mechanism or a restructure, and any round where the Implementer had
to choose between legitimate treatments. T020B's R1 was that round - state a
rule for unaligned windows or refuse them at setup - and it needed the stronger
reader.

**A fresh Claude `assetops-reviewer` reviews verification.** A correction round
where an independent reviewer has already named the defects and the fix needs
no design choice: prose, docstrings, claim corrections, test assertions,
deletions. The job is confirming the named items closed and nothing else moved.
Fresh rather than resumed, because there is no context worth preserving for
checking named sentences and a clean reader is worth more.

The honest risk, recorded so it is not discovered later: this relies on the
previous review having found everything discoverable in that area. Four
consecutive rounds on T020B each found something the last missed, which is the
argument against cheap re-reviews. **So a Claude verification pass that finds
something unexpected is a signal to escalate that round back to Codex, not a
cheap verdict to accept.** Finding an unnamed defect means the round was
discovery after all and was misclassified.

Two classification traps worth naming, both seen on T020B:

- **A correction round can widen under the Implementer's hand.** The R1a/R1b
  round was three sentences and a deletion when dispatched; it came back having
  also corrected an unnamed fourth sentence and added a property-based guard
  over thirteen windows. Classify on what came back, not on what was sent.
- **"Small" will drift to "everything" if it is not tested against the two
  lists above.** The question is not how many lines changed. It is whether a
  reader had to decide anything.

## Owner Review Routing From S3 Onward (2026-10-11)

Owner (verbatim, `.agent/owner-review-routing-and-merge-2026-10-11.md`): "codex usage optimizaion is important, so keep the same arragment for S2 going forward". For every slice from S3 onward: a fresh Claude `assetops-reviewer` does the slice's first review and every re-review; small written-list fix rounds run on a Sonnet implementer; one batched Codex review covers each milestone's slices together at the milestone's end; Codex Architect/Planner questions are batched rather than sent per question file. This overrides "Codex reviews discovery" below for per-slice first reviews; Codex remains the Architect, the Planner and the milestone reviewer.

## Do Not Write The Review In The Brief

Recorded on 2026-09-26 at the user's instruction, because this project had
been doing the opposite. Reviewer briefs had grown to eight numbered questions,

`gpt-6-sol` is the workhorse coding model; `gpt-6-luna` is the cheap one. Full
list: `gpt-6-astra`, `gpt-6-sol`, `gpt-6-luna`, `gpt-5.6-sol`, `gpt-5.6-terra`,
`gpt-5.6-luna`, `gpt-5.5`.

Reserve Astra for genuine architecture and review judgement. Mechanical passes
- a fix round against a written list, a routine Planner expansion, a
consistency sweep - do not need it.

### Never spend a full session on a probe

`~/.codex/config.toml` sets `model_reasoning_effort = "high"` globally, while
the models' own default is `low`. A one-line availability probe therefore runs
at high reasoning. If you must probe, use
`-m gpt-6-luna -c model_reasoning_effort=low`. Better: attempt the real work
and detect the limit from the error, which costs nothing when the limit is
already hit.

### Put verification in the brief, not in the session

Every fact the brief states is work the session does not repeat. Say what you
already ran and what you already verified - test counts, check results,
confirmed line numbers - and say plainly that repeating it is not wanted.
Ask Codex for judgement, not for re-running a green suite.

### Invocation mechanics that have already gone wrong

- **Pass the prompt as an argument, never on stdin.** `codex exec ... - < file`
  blocked for three hours with no output on 2026-09-23. Always redirect
  `< /dev/null`.
- **Do not double-background.** `nohup codex ... &` inside an already
  backgrounded call returns instantly, and the harness reports a completion
  that has not happened. Background the call once.
- **A branch carries a snapshot of these rules.** A session started on a task
  branch reads that branch's `.ai/`, not main's. Merge main into the branch
  before a review if a rule has changed.

## Using The Claude Implementer Efficiently

Set by the owner on 2026-09-29 after T023, T022B and T024 each cost 80-200M
re-read tokens and one to three hours. Measured from the transcripts: cost is
context size times turns, and context grew from resumed agents and from source
code read whole into context. The agent's own rules are in
`.claude/agents/assetops-implementer.md` "Context budget"; these are the
coordinator's:

- **A fresh implementer per build and per fix round. Never resume one.**
  T024's build started at 209k tokens because the read-only planning agent was
  resumed; T023's fix rounds started at 458k. If a slice needs a planning pass,
  the planner writes its plan to `.agent/T0NN-implementer-plan.md` and a fresh
  agent builds from that file.
- **Fix rounds run on Sonnet** (`model: sonnet` on dispatch), briefed with the
  review file and the findings to fix. New slices stay on the session model,
  where design judgement matters.
- **Keep slices small.** A task the implementer sizes at several times a
  previous slice is a split candidate before it is built, not after.
- **Build large slices in phases, a fresh agent per phase.** Set by the owner
  on 2026-09-29 after T025a: bounded reads cut read volume by about two thirds,
  but 508 turns still took context to 467k and cost 142M re-read tokens,
  because cost grows with the square of the turn count. A slice beyond one
  focused change is dispatched as phases (for example contracts and core, then
  model and setup, then Lab and frontend). Each phase ends with committed work
  and a short handoff at `.agent/T0NN-phase-N-handoff.md` (what landed, file
  anchors, what is next, open risks); the coordinator dispatches a fresh agent
  for the next phase from that file. Full suites and the packet belong to the
  last phase.
- **Hand off at about 120k context.** Set by the owner on 2026-10-02 after
  usage showed 66% of tokens spent above 150k context and 67% in implementer
  subagents. An implementer that nears 120k commits what is safe, writes its
  handoff and stops, even mid-phase; a fresh agent continues from the handoff.
  The coordinator plans phases so that none is expected to reach that size,
  splitting a phase into sub-phases (2a, 2b) rather than letting one run long.
- **Backend tests: focused per phase, full once per slice** (OPS-01,
  `backend/TESTING.md`). Each phase runs the focused and adjacent backend files
  it touched; the real-Postgres full suite runs once per slice, on the final
  revision, with the guide's normal command and JUnit zero-skip check.
  Reviewers (Claude or Codex) cite that same-revision full evidence and run
  focused files; they rerun the full suite only for a finding that needs it.
  Before any full run start `tools/keep-awake-during-tests.ps1` and cap the run
  at twice its expected wall time; a run past the cap is stopped and reported,
  never waited on. One full suite on this machine at a time.
- **Mechanical sub-steps run on Sonnet.** Work fixed by an agreed contract or
  handoff - tests written against a settled interface, an AC-by-AC evidence
  pass, closeout records - is dispatched with `model: sonnet`, like fix rounds.
  Steps that decide a design stay on the session model.

## Updating Bindings

Change bindings here when the team wants a different tool to own a role. Record
material changes in `.ai/DECISIONS.md` if they affect workflow, review
independence, or task ownership.

## Arithmetic spot check (cold, Euler per minute, r = 1 - 0.08/72)
- `excursion` checks out. From 3.80 degC, T(19:15) = 20 - 16.2*r^15 = 4.068, publishing 4.1. T(19:20) = 4.156, publishing 4.2. T(19:10) = 3.979, publishing 4.0, which is in band. So the start is bounded [19:10,19:15], the latest start is 19:15, and the open episode lower bound at 20:00 is 45 min, which is at least 15. The failed result at 19:05 matches the 5-minute timeout.
- `missing-probe`: 7/13 is about 0.54. `late-reading`: 12/13 is about 0.92 at the 20:05 cutoff. When 19:20 arrives late, the degree-minute bounds change (10-minute vs 5-minute rectangles). Start bounds stay the same, so the anchor holds.
- `normal` holds numerically, but the rationale given is wrong (L1).
- The EV numbers also hold: 30.000, 15.000, 10.000 and 25.000 kWh, and 300 s.

## New Medium findings (cold; both block cold only)

**R-M1 - The event INSERT's conflict target breaks message dedup for EVENT rows.** (W2-00; cold lines 38, 134, 136)
- Today, message dedup relies only on `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT accepted_evidence_message_unique DO NOTHING` (`postgres_evidence_ledger.py:482`). `ledger_service.py` has no message precheck.
- The design gives EVENT inserts the event-identity partial target instead. It also says "a unique violation on another constraint is not converted". So a retransmitted event message (same message ID, in a different batch) raises a message-unique violation rather than returning `DUPLICATE` or `MESSAGE_CONFLICT`, which the design itself promises at line 22.
- Precedence is also unspecified when a record collides on both the message key and the event identity.
- Fix: keep the message arbiter on every INSERT. Detect event-identity and session-START collisions with a savepoint and reread, with a fixed order: message, then event, then session. Alternatively, precheck under a named advisory lock. Then restate the exact `$ledgerConflictAllowed` regex and the probes to match.

**R-M2 - The cold vendor peer's raw format is unnamed.** (cold line 157, W2-02 proof at line 163)
- `vendor-format-cold-peer/1` "maps an independent raw-format fixture" but names no raw format, units, module, fixture path or refusal codes. This is the same implementer-chosen contract that EV M6 returned.
- Fix: name the format, units, module, path and codes, or defer the peer to the pilot gate as EV does. Deferring is consistent with demo speed, and the line-163 proof would need updating.

## Low (non-blocking)
- L1 `normal` (line 82): the "three powerless minutes" rationale misses the thermostat cycle. Passive warming takes T to 4.008 at 19:55, which publishes 4.0. The compressor runs from 19:56, so 20:00 publishes 3.9. The no-Finding claim holds only by a 0.008 degC margin and strict `>`. State this, and also state the transfer and generator behaviour after `GRID_RESTORE` at 19:35, which is unspecified.
- L2 Range notation differs between the designs. Cold `19:15-19:40` is inclusive on point times (6 points dropped). EV `08:10-08:15` is one slot. The cold window is `[19:00,20:00)` yet counts the 20:00 point among its 13. Pick one convention.
- L3 EV names `consequence_worker.py` (line 105), but the existing worker is `financials/recompute.py:216`.
- L4 Cold and EV work rows use `UPDATE status`. The fuel path instead uses append-only `financial_consequence_work_attempt`. This is concrete but a divergence, so confirm it is intended.
- L5 Cold line 36 says 0025 widens "both" domain CHECKs. EV line 12 widens only `finding.domain`. Name the `finding_revision` widening in EV.
- L6 Cold roles still say "ONE per attempt" (lines 60, 63), and the cold `fault`/CLEAR plugged state is unspecified in EV. Hold/drop/fault are new to `observation/transform.py`, which today only has bias and dropout. Name the module.

## 1. W2-00: ordinary source-to-evidence format

The active authenticated path uses **SourceEnvelopeV3**, not the older staged v1/v2 envelope: v3 names record families but refuses them, while S2 also refuses non-measurements (`contracts/assetops_contracts/source_envelope_v3.py:1-43,77-78,240-246`; `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/ledger.py:268-280`). Extend that path as schema **4**, `assetops.source-envelope`; retain v3's allocated `(gateway,source,epoch,sequence)` message identity, canonical JSON, UTC instants and exact numeric text. `source_envelope.py` v1/v2 and existing Stage artifacts remain byte-for-byte readable. The v4 parser accepts v4 only; a version dispatcher accepts v3 and v4. v3 receipts, dedup keys and accepted rows are untouched. A new publication profile pins v4; never switch an existing profile in place (`contracts/assetops_contracts/source_envelope_v3.py:1-43,70-78`; `simulator/assetops_simulator/gateway/mappings.py:87-107`).

| v4 family / kind | Exact payload keys in addition to common v3 top-level fields | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `MEASUREMENT` | Exact v4 keys `family,measurement,value,unit,quality,unavailable_reason,session_id,connector_id`; the last two are explicit null for cold and non-session EV channels. v4 alone permits `STATE_AT_INSTANT`,`INTERVAL_MEAN`,`INTERVAL_DELTA`; delta is nonnegative `kWh` over a positive `[interval_start,interval_end)` and requires both EV IDs. Cold point Â°C uses `degC`; states use `state`; mean power uses `kW`. | Define a separate `V4_MEASUREMENTS` allowlist in `source_envelope_v4.py`; leave shared `ENVELOPE_MEASUREMENTS` and v3 parsing unchanged. Null value only with `UNAVAILABLE`; `SUSPECT` excluded from assertions. |
| `EVENT` / `DOOR_OPEN`, `DOOR_CLOSE`, `START_REQUEST`, `START_RESULT`, `TRANSFER_RESULT`, `POWER_LOSS`, `POWER_RESTORED`, `MANUAL_LOG` | `family,record_kind,event_id,correlation_id,component_id,actor,outcome,unit,value,quality`. `event_id` and `correlation_id` required identifiers; `component_id` required except Site power events; `actor` = `CONTROLLER`,`SENSOR`,`OPERATOR`; `outcome` = `REQUESTED`,`SUCCEEDED`,`FAILED`,`OPEN`,`CLOSED`,`LOST`,`RESTORED`,`OBSERVED`,`UNKNOWN`, constrained per kind; `unit/value` either both null or a permitted quantitative pair (manual log `degC` only); `quality` = `GOOD` or `SUSPECT`. | An event is a witnessed public observation, not private disturbance truth. `MANUAL_LOG` needs registered operator source and stays contextual unless calibrated to the probe role. |
| `EVENT` / `EV_SESSION_START`, `EV_SESSION_END`, `EV_SESSION_ABORT`, `EV_CONNECTOR_FAULT`, `EV_CONNECTOR_CLEAR` | Same keys plus `session_id,connector_id`; both required for session kinds, `connector_id` required for fault/clear and `session_id` nullable there. `outcome` = `STARTED`,`COMPLETED`,`ABORTED`,`FAULTED`,`CLEARED`; `value/unit` null. | Session identity scoped to `(tenant,site,charger asset,connector asset,session_id)`; no vehicle/customer identity. |

Kind-to-outcome allowlist is exact: `DOOR_OPENâ†’OPEN`, `DOOR_CLOSEâ†’CLOSED`, `START_REQUESTâ†’REQUESTED`, `START_RESULTâ†’SUCCEEDED|FAILED`, `TRANSFER_RESULTâ†’SUCCEEDED|FAILED`, `POWER_LOSSâ†’LOST`, `POWER_RESTOREDâ†’RESTORED`, `MANUAL_LOGâ†’OBSERVED`, `EV_SESSION_STARTâ†’STARTED`, `EV_SESSION_ENDâ†’COMPLETED`, `EV_SESSION_ABORTâ†’ABORTED`, `EV_CONNECTOR_FAULTâ†’FAULTED`, `EV_CONNECTOR_CLEARâ†’CLEARED`. All other pairs refuse `INVALID_PAYLOAD`. `MANUAL_LOG` requires value/`degC`; every other event requires both null. A record kind with no `session_id` still carries the key as null; `connector_id` is likewise explicit null for cold events.

Every v4 top-level object keeps the v3 exact keys; `message` keeps `message_id,sequence_epoch,sequence`, `source` and `mapping` keep v3 keys, `timing` keeps `source_time,published_at,interval_start,interval_end`. Events require null interval bounds; their source time is the observed transition, not a duration. For cold event `correlation_id` is the controller outage/attempt ID; a door pair shares its door-cycle ID. For EV, `correlation_id=session_id` on session events and optional registered fault episode ID on fault/clear. `event_id` is stable under retransmission and unique within `(site,source,record_kind)`; message ID remains transport identity. Canonical event payload contains explicit nulls for inapplicable fields. No arbitrary JSON detail, free text, Lab run ID, oracle, private cause, or stock identity. Event IDs generated from frozen scenario + public device channel + transition ordinal in Lab; physical adapters preserve vendor stable event ID or derive a documented hash from raw immutable event key. Cross-source event correlation is a claim input, never an acceptance dedup key.

| Boundary | Exact change / refusal |
| --- | --- |
| Parser and mapper | Add `source_envelope_v4.py` and golden vectors; keep v3 code frozen. Extend mapping entry with `allowed_record_kinds`, `allowed_outcomes`, `requires_correlation`, `requires_session`, `allowed_units`; v3 mapping snapshots parse without these keys. New `simulator-lab-gateway-cold` v1 only allows the channel list in Â§4. Refuse `SCHEMA_UNSUPPORTED`, `MISSING_FIELD`, `UNKNOWN_FIELD`, `WRONG_TYPE`, `INVALID_IDENTITY`, `INVALID_TIMING`, `INVALID_PAYLOAD`, `NOT_CANONICAL`, `MAPPING_INCOMPATIBLE`, `UNIT_INCOMPATIBLE`, `RECORD_KIND_UNSUPPORTED`, `CORRELATION_INVALID`. A v4 kind under v3 is `SCHEMA_UNSUPPORTED`. |
| S2 intake | Dispatch by stated schema only; validate auth scope, pinned binding revision, role/asset/kind/unit and source time within Foundation/binding validity before accept. Same message ID + same SHA is `DUPLICATE`; same message ID + different SHA remains byte-stable `MESSAGE_CONFLICT`/`CONFLICT`. Different SHA at existing `(tenant,site,source,record_kind,event_id)` is `EVENT_ID_CONFLICT`/`CONFLICT`; a second distinct EV START for `(tenant,site,connector asset,session_id)` is `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT`/`CONFLICT`. Each conflict sets `conflicts_with_evidence_id` to the first accepted record. Identical event under a new message ID is `DUPLICATE` pointing to that event. `EV_CONNECTOR_MISMATCH`, `EV_DELTA_UNIT_INVALID`, `EV_DELTA_INTERVAL_INVALID`, `EV_DELTA_VALUE_INVALID` are `REJECTED`, with null conflict reference. Every refusal writes `evidence_rejection` and one ordinal disposition. Receipt V2 `conflicts` counts all three conflict kinds; `rejected` counts the four EV codes and other refusals. Distinct conflicting terminal events remain accepted for analytic contradiction (`backend/assetops_backend/evidence/ledger_service.py:79-167`; `backend/migrations/versions/0005_evidence_ledger.py:161-216`). |
| Accepted storage/read | `accepted_evidence.family` becomes `MEASUREMENT|EVENT`; for EVENT, `measurement,conversion_kind` become nullable, while event quality remains `GOOD|SUSPECT`; original/normalized value and unit null unless quantitative manual log. Typed event columns store identity/kind/correlation/actor/outcome, not merely envelope JSON. Keep exact `envelope_text`, SHA, accepted-as Foundation/binding, received/published/source times and S4-05 publication lineage. `/evidence/records` adds `family,record_kind,event_id,correlation_id,session_id,connector_id,outcome` and family/kind filters; old records serialize nulls. No event is converted into a fake state reading (`backend/migrations/versions/0005_evidence_ledger.py:161-216`; `backend/assetops_backend/evidence_api.py:54-59`). |

## 2. Schema and migration ledger

Postgres migration head is `0019_verification_core` (`backend/migrations/versions/0019_verification_core.py:1-29`). All new rows carry `(tenant_id,site_id)` and composite same-Site FKs. Use `ledger_append_only()` for facts/revisions, and the existing S1 effective/recorded validity rules for Foundation and binding publications (`backend/migrations/versions/0005_evidence_ledger.py:161-216`; `backend/migrations/versions/0007_dispatch_assessment.py:83-125`). No data backfill invents events or observations.

| --- | --- | --- |
| `0020_w2_source_event` (W2-00) | `accepted_evidence`: nullable `record_kind text`, `event_id text`, `correlation_id text`, `session_id text`, `connector_id text`, `actor text`, `outcome text`; relax `measurement`, `conversion_kind`, `original_unit`, `normalized_unit` to nullable; keep `quality` NOT NULL. `0022` adds nullable `finding.subject_asset_id uuid` with scoped asset FK; relaxes `finding.generator_asset_id` to nullable; adds CHECK `(domain='mini_grid' AND generator_asset_id IS NOT NULL) OR (domain='cold_chain' AND subject_asset_id IS NOT NULL AND generator_asset_id IS NULL)`. It also adds `finding_revision.domain text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'mini_grid'` for existing rows, drops the default after backfill, adds UNIQUE `(tenant_id,site_id,id,domain)` on `finding`, and replaces the revision's parent FK with `(tenant_id,site_id,finding_id,domain)` to that unique key. Thus revision CHECKs can use their own stored domain without trusting a caller. `0025` widens both domain CHECKs with `'ev_charging'`. One literal per domain is used in Finding, FindingRevision, `recompute_job.domain`, and episode/session producers: `mini_grid`, `cold_chain`, `ev_charging`. Existing `mini_grid` rows stay untouched apart from the additive revision domain backfill. `recompute_job.domain` has no SQL CHECK. `0022` adds the `(tenant_id,site_id,domain,subject_asset_id,anchor_start)` index and updates Finding store/read selection by domain. The shared fuel consequence CHECK remains intact. The `0020` accepted-event unique index is enforced in the ledger transaction; race losers get the conflict disposition above.

For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. The ledger INSERT uses the event-identity partial conflict target; a unique violation on `accepted_ev_start_unique` rolls back that record's savepoint, rereads the first START, and emits `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT` in the same batch transaction. A unique violation on another constraint is not converted into this code. The session unique index is the race arbiter, not an application precheck.

## 3. W2-01 Foundation and binding contract

Role set `cold-roles-1` is an immutable named version in `sites/binding_roles.py`; register in `ROLE_SET_BY_SITE_TYPE` and `SERVED_ROLE_SETS_BY_SITE_TYPE` (`backend/assetops_backend/sites/binding_roles.py:17-27,117-130`). The values below are canonical. `ONE` means one binding per Site, `PER_ASSET` one per named asset. All source/device/signal rows must be registered and linked to the same Site and effective Foundation revision. No binding may silently cover two incompatible roles. Numeric normalization is IDENTITY in v1; state ENUM needs an explicit finite mapping; Celsius conversion is not implicit.

| Role / asset type | Kind, unit, cardinality | Need |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `chamber_temperature` / `TEMPERATURE_PROBE` | point number `degC`, PER_ASSET | required, one calibrated probe per `COLD_ROOM` via declared probe-to-room link |
| `cold_policy_low`,`cold_policy_high` / `COLD_ROOM` | Foundation control number `degC`, PER_ASSET | required; low < high, same effective revision |
| `compressor_state` / `COMPRESSOR` | point state `OFF|STARTING|RUNNING|FAILED`, PER_ASSET | required for cooling claim |
| `compressor_power` / `COMPRESSOR` | interval mean `kW`, PER_ASSET | optional corroboration |
| `grid_state` / `METER` | point state `AVAILABLE|LOST`, ONE | required for outage step |
| `backup_request`,`backup_result` / `GENERATOR` | EVENT `START_REQUEST`,`START_RESULT`, ONE per attempt | required for start-response claim |
| `generator_state` / `GENERATOR` | point state `STOPPED|STARTING|RUNNING|FAILED`, ONE | required for observed running state |
| `backup_bus_state` / `AC_BUS` | point state `POWERED|UNPOWERED`, ONE | required for supply-to-cooling claim |
| `transfer_result` / `TRANSFER_SWITCH` | EVENT `TRANSFER_RESULT`, ONE per attempt | optional: absent blocks switch-position claim |
| `door_event` / `COLD_ROOM` | EVENT open/close, PER_ASSET | optional context |
| `ambient_temperature` / `TEMPERATURE_PROBE` | point number `degC`, PER_ASSET | optional context, never substitutes chamber probe |
| `manual_temperature` / `COLD_ROOM` | EVENT manual log `degC`, PER_ASSET | optional context; never silently closes a probe gap |

Foundation topology uses explicit `COLD_ROOM`â†’`TEMPERATURE_PROBE`, `COMPRESSOR`â†’room, busâ†’compressor, generatorâ†’transferâ†’bus relations; every referenced asset must exist, one chamber probe cannot be primary for two rooms, and cycles/duplicate edges are refused. Policy band belongs to a room and is effective for the selected window; changing it creates a Foundation revision and binding set selection, never an in-place update. Validate finite exact positive thermal parameters, `0<=hysteresis<high-low`, probe error `>=0`, timeout `>0`, and cadence 5 minutes for the first Lab profile. Publication readiness must say **â€œAdd a chamber temperature probe and bind its Â°C readingâ€**, **â€œSet a lower and upper temperature policy for this roomâ€**, **â€œBind the generator start request and resultâ€**, or **â€œTransfer position is not reported; switch state is unknownâ€** as applicable. Configuration readiness, commissioning evidence and gateway contact remain separate (`backend/assetops_backend/sites/binding_service.py:267-317,674-715`; `.agent/architect-W2-design.md:32-53`).

## 4. W2-02 causal producer and publication

Add `cold-thermal/1` `ModelSpec` and `cold-publication/1`; resolve frozen profile identity in `host/execution_adapter.py:113-140`, `host/lab_execution.py:1978-2015` and `simulator/assetops_simulator/kernel/execute.py:90-105` through a closed registry, with fuel/mini-grid identities preserved. Reject unknown pack/profile/parameter version before Draft. The kernel receives typed model laws and power ports, not a cold-specific branch. Lab freezes Foundation/binding IDs, scenario SHA, model/profile/observation versions and seed in run identity; Stage seals public v4 envelopes, registered Commit links publication, SQLite outbox sends authenticated Batch V2, and S2 alone creates accepted evidence (`host/lab_execution.py:2154-2164,2428-2501`; `.agent/architect-W2-design.md:55-66`).

**Time and state.** Fixed 1-minute UTC step; all updates use exact rationals/decimal text and a defined end-of-step publication order. State: chamber air temperature `T` degC, controller demand, compressor `OFF|STARTING|RUNNING|FAILED`, grid/backup bus energized flags, generator `STOPPED|STARTING|RUNNING|FAILED`, transfer position `GRID|BACKUP|OPEN`, door `OPEN|CLOSED`, probe `OK|FAILED`. Private forcing: ambient `Ta`, door schedule, grid loss, generator failure, probe failure; private truth never serializes into a product envelope. Initial `T=3Â°C`, `Ta=20Â°C`, door closed, compressor off, grid and bus on, generator stopped, transfer grid, probe OK. Per minute, `heat_kWh = (heat_leak_kW_per_C*(Ta-T) + door_open*door_heat_kWh*60)*1/60`; `cool_kWh = compressor_running_and_bus_powered*cooling_kW*1/60`; `T_next=T+(heat_kWh-cool_kWh)/thermal_capacity_kWh_per_C`. Defaults: thermal capacity `1.2 kWh/degC`, leak `0.08 kW/degC`, door heat `0.02 kWh/min` (stored as `1.2 kW` equivalent while open), cooling `3 kW`, compressor start delay 1 minute, generator start delay 2 minutes, transfer delay 1 minute, start timeout 5 minutes, control on `T>=4Â°C`, off `T<=2Â°C`, policy `[2,4]Â°C`, hysteresis 0.2Â°C, probe bound Â±0.1Â°C. Clamp no state: fail run if `T` outside `[-40,60]Â°C`, energy residual nonzero, negative capacity/power, or impossible transition. The standalone cold pack owns its generator and bus power port; W2-02 does not compose with mini-grid.

Grid loss opens transfer and deenergizes compressor bus; controller emits `START_REQUEST` only if backup enabled, generator reaches RUNNING after delay unless failed, transfer closes to BACKUP after delay if generator power exists, and compressor restarts after its own delay if controller still demands cooling. Failed start emits `START_RESULT/FAILED` at timeout; successful start emits `START_RESULT/SUCCEEDED` when running. Transfer result is emitted only if a position sensor is configured; absence stays absence. Door events are sensor transitions; door opening adds heat only while open. Probe failure emits one `UNAVAILABLE` reading with reason `SENSOR_FAILURE` if the profile publishes it, then no numeric sample; reporting-path loss emits no envelope. Device measurement is `T + fixed observation bias` (default 0), rounded to 0.1Â°C by half-even rule; no private cause field. Controller and power events use observed public transitions and stable correlation ID.

All six `config/scenarios/cold-*.yaml` use `cold-scenario/1`, UTC window 2026-10-06 19:00â€“20:00, seed 20417, one standalone `cold-thermal/1` pack, and no mini-grid composition. Assets are `room-1`, `probe-1`, `door-1`, `generator-1`, `grid-1`, `bus-1`, `compressor-1`; generator backup enabled. Base initial state is T=3.00Â°C, ambient=20Â°C, door CLOSED, compressor OFF, grid/bus POWERED, generator STOPPED, transfer GRID, probe OK; excursion-derived cases override initial T to 3.80Â°C. Each row lists the entire non-default forcing, ordered by `(time,kind,asset)`; observation faults occur after a valid world transition and cannot bypass `PhysicallyUnacceptable` guards.

| Pack | UTC forcing `(time,kind,asset/connector,value)`; door schedule | Public assertion in 19:00â€“20:00 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `normal` | `(19:05,GRID_LOSS,grid-1,LOST)`, `(19:35,GRID_RESTORE,grid-1,AVAILABLE)`; door CLOSED throughout. | Request 19:05, success 19:07, transfer 19:08, then bus powered; max passive warming during three powerless minutes is <0.05Â°C from 3.00Â°C, so no excursion Finding. |
| `excursion` | `(19:00,GRID_LOSS,grid-1,LOST)`, `(19:00,GENERATOR_FAILURE,generator-1,FAILED)`; door CLOSED throughout; initial T=3.80Â°C. | Failed result 19:05; T at 19:15 >4.06Â°C and at 19:20 >4.15Â°C; first two GOOD out-of-band samples qualify, latest start 19:15, open episode at 20:00 has duration lower bound 45 min, exceeding 15 min. |
| `backup-failed` | Same forcing and initial state as `excursion`; `(19:10,DOOR_OPEN,door-1,OPEN)`, `(19:11,DOOR_CLOSE,door-1,CLOSED)`. | Explicit failed start plus unpowered bus and compressor establish `OBSERVED_FAILURE`; door adds 0.02 kWh (<0.017Â°C) and remains contextual, exposure Finding still qualifies. |
| `missing-probe` | Same world forcing/initial state as `excursion`; observation drop `(19:15â€“19:40,PROBE_REPORT,probe-1,DROP)`; door CLOSED. | Only 7 of 13 scheduled temperature points remain, coverage 7/13 <0.8; temperature claim `UNKNOWN`, no supported exposure Finding; start failure remains observed. |
| `late-reading` | Same world forcing/initial state as `excursion`; observation hold `(19:20,PROBE_REPORT,probe-1,RELEASE_AT_20:10)`; door CLOSED. | At first cutoff 20:05, 12/13 points remain and first two high samples are 19:15/19:25; at 20:10 the accepted 19:20 point enqueues `cold_chain` recompute, changes exact evidence basis and bounds, appends a revision without rewriting the old one. |
| `contradictory` | Same world forcing/initial state as `excursion`; observation fault `(19:05,START_RESULT,generator-1,SUCCEEDED)` emits an additional distinct public controller event ID beside the genuine FAILED event, both GOOD and same correlation ID; door CLOSED. | Both events are individually valid and accepted; start-response claim is `CONTRADICTORY`, never `OBSERVED_FAILURE`. The frozen observation profile, not the physical model, creates the conflicting report. |

`cold-publication/1` emits chamber `STATE_AT_INSTANT degC` every 5 minutes including end; grid/generator/compressor/bus states on transitions and 5-minute heartbeat; compressor `INTERVAL_MEAN kW` per aligned 5-minute slot; door/start/transfer/power events on transition. `cold-observation/1` freezes the three specified hold/drop/fault variants, zero bias and default 3-second latency before Draft. The model retains one calibrated primary chamber probe per room; delete the two-primary-probe disagreement rule. Contradictory distinct GOOD controller outcomes for one attempt cause `CONTRADICTORY`. `cold-door-heat` property unit is `kWh/min`, with 0.02 per open minute; the equation's `*60` converts it to kW before its one-minute integration. The Lab persisted-state validator uses the `cold-thermal/1` finite vocabularies above, not the mini-grid generator lifecycle vocabulary; extend the closed identity/profile registries. `Role.value_kind` adds `EVENT`, and EVENT bindings use `conversion='IDENTITY'`, with typed outcome allowlist and no numeric conversion. Foundation controls stay NUMBER, not a new `Role.value_kind`.

The closed cold mapping uses `(device_id,signal_id)`: `(chamber-probe,temperature)` degC point, `(ambient-probe,temperature)` degC point, `(grid-meter,state)` state point, `(generator-controller,state)` state point, `(compressor-controller,state)` state point, `(backup-bus-meter,state)` state point, `(compressor-meter,power)` kW interval mean, `(door-sensor,event)` door events, `(generator-controller,start-event)` request/result events, `(transfer-controller,event)` transfer result, `(grid-meter,event)` loss/restored events, `(operator-log,temperature-event)` manual log. Each tuple is a separate versioned mapping entry with only its listed kinds, values and cadence. W2-00 also reserves EV entries `(charger-controller,session-event)` for session start/end/abort and `(connector-controller,fault-event)` for fault/clear, with connector/session identity rules above; W2-05b supplies the EV pack and its own frozen mapping version. The same signal ID on a different device is not an implicit alias (`simulator/assetops_simulator/gateway/mappings.py:22-46,87-107`).

## 5. W2-03 assessment, episodes and Findings

Analytic IDs `cold-excursion/1`, `cold-start-response/1`. Input selection is accepted evidence only, fixed half-open `[from,to)`, explicit `known_at` cutoff on server `received_at`/decision time, effective Foundation/binding versions, and source gate. Include exact accepted IDs and SHA, configuration IDs, model version, window/cutoff in sorted basis SHA. A reading is usable only if GOOD, calibrated, mapped to the room, and clock-attested; SUSPECT is context. Expected five-minute points: coverage = usable distinct scheduled timestamps / scheduled timestamps, capped at 1; `>=0.8` needed for an interval claim, but no gap >10 minutes may be bridged. Latest point must be <=10 minutes old at read time for a **current** state; historical window conclusion can remain bounded with explicit as-of. A failed worker or newer accepted relevant evidence marks prior projection `PENDING_RECOMPUTE` until a new immutable revision is written.

Excursion begins after two consecutive GOOD observations above upper band (or below lower band) at least 5 minutes apart; return requires two consecutive observations inside band by at least 0.2Â°C. The start is bounded by `[last in-band, first out-of-band]`; return by `[last out-of-band, first stable in-band]`; duration low = max(0, earliest return - latest start), high = latest return - earliest start, computed only across continuously covered spans. Initial out-of-band sample with no prior in-band gives unknown start; window end without return gives open episode and no finite high. `degree_minutes` bounds use observed rectangles only between adjacent usable samples <=10 minutes apart: low uses min adjacent exceedance, high max adjacent exceedance times interval minutes; add no unobserved gap area. Peak is the maximum **observed** value. An exposure Finding requires 15-minute lower duration and coverage >=0.8; otherwise `POSSIBLE` or `UNKNOWN`, never â€œno excursionâ€ from missing samples. In-band fully covered is `NO_EXCURSION_OBSERVED` scoped to window, not equipment health.

Start chain groups by accepted `correlation_id`, generator asset and outage window. Require ordered grid LOST â†’ START_REQUEST â†’ START_RESULT â†’ generator RUNNING/FAILED â†’ optional TRANSFER_RESULT â†’ bus POWERED/UNPOWERED â†’ compressor state â†’ probe response; tolerance is source-clock bound <=30 seconds for event ordering, with timeout from effective Foundation (default 5 minutes). A failed automatic start is `OBSERVED_FAILURE` only with request, explicit failed result or timeout plus complete generator state coverage through timeout, and no contradictory success; missing request/result/state is `UNKNOWN_STEP`. A transfer-position claim requires accepted transfer event; bus energization alone proves only bus state. Cooling failure cannot be assigned from temperature alone. Emit claim levels `OBSERVED`, `BOUNDED`, `POSSIBLE`, `UNKNOWN`, `CONTRADICTORY`; explanations list exact missing/excluded records and claim ceiling.

Episode key = SHA-256 of `(tenant,site,kind,subject room/generator asset,first accepted qualifying crossing/request event ID)`; retain anchor when late evidence adjusts boundaries, append `cold_episode_revision` and corresponding FindingRevision with transition `SUPPORT_CHANGED` or `BASIS_REFRESHED`. Two episodes split only after two stable in-band points (excursion) or a new start request (backup). If late evidence invalidates the anchor, append `NO_LONGER_SUPPORTED` and create a new episode with `split_from` relation; never rewrite old. Separate excursion and start-response Findings can cite the same event but share one `overlap_group` for money. Use existing S4-05 exact accepted-ID provenance and source classification (`backend/migrations/versions/0009_finding_store.py:33-87`; `.ai/DECISIONS.md:5-15`).

## 6. W2-04 consequence, reads and UI

`PRODUCT_EXPOSURE` is a bounded thermal exposure indicator in `degC_min`, **not product quantity, spoilage or loss**. `cold_stock_quantity`, `cold_stock_unit_value` (currency/unit) and a product-specific reviewed `cold_exposure_rule` would be needed for a future valued risk scenario; W2 creates no default and still forbids an amount without an approved rule. `RECORDED_REPAIR` amount requires exact accepted work/cost record, currency, time and non-overlap; no such source exists in W2, so it is `UNPRICED`. All consequences pin FindingRevision, episode revision, accepted IDs, assumption revision IDs, model/formula version, source gate and overlap group. Missing/contradictory probe or policy, stale projection, mixed/unknown provenance, undeclared stock or cost â†’ `UNPRICED`/`WITHHELD` with null amount. Never sum backup and excursion exposure; no zero amount as stand-in for unknown. The existing LR current-money and S4-05 source gates still apply (`backend/migrations/versions/0010_financial_assumptions.py:1-30`; `backend/migrations/versions/0011_financial_consequences.py:43-69`; `.ai/DECISIONS.md:5-15`).

All routes below use Site code, authenticated tenant scope, UTC `from,to` (end exclusive), optional UTC `known_at` (default current committed watermark), max 31-day window, and return `{site_id,window:{from,to,known_at},currentness:{state,watermark,latest_relevant_evidence_at,pending_since},source_provenance,limitations,...}`. `watermark` is latest included ingestion disposition `decided_at` plus assessment revision ID; a selected historical cutoff cannot claim today's currentness. Lists use opaque `(time,id)` cursor, `limit` default 50 max 200 and `{items,next_cursor}`. Shared refusal envelope `{error:{code,message}}`: 401 unauthenticated, 403 wrong tenant/source-gated disclosure, 404 Site/record not found or wrong type, 409 unresolved selected revision or recompute conflict, 422 malformed/window/range/unknown parameter, 503 ledger/projection unavailable; a valid missing claim returns 200 with `UNKNOWN`/null, never 404 or a fabricated zero. Existing evidence/Finding paths continue to serve exact links (`backend/assetops_backend/evidence_api.py:157-161`; `backend/assetops_backend/finding_api.py:102-189`).

## 7. Slice proof fixtures and acceptance

### Writer guard and money trigger contract

Rule 8 in `tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1` is extended exactly: add `cold_episode,cold_episode_revision,cold_episode_evidence` to `$assessTables` before `$assessInserts` is derived; add `cold_consequence_revision,cold_consequence_evidence` to `$consequenceTables`; add `cold_consequence_work` to a new `$consequenceWorkTables` array. `postgres_assessment_store.py` alone inserts episode and analytic/assessment rows; `postgres_finding_store.py` alone inserts Findings. `assessment_worker.py` owns one caller-supplied transaction spanning both adapters and job completion, commits after both have inserted, and retries the whole transaction on serializable conflict. `postgres_consequence_store.py` alone inserts cold consequence rows and leases/completes work. In the consequence writer branch, allow `INSERT` only into `$consequenceTables`, and `UPDATE cold_consequence_work SET status=...,lease_owner=...,lease_until=...,updated_at=... WHERE id=...` with assigned columns limited to those four; reject runtime `INSERT` into work and all other UPDATE/DELETE. In the cross-writer branch, `$consequenceTables + $consequenceWorkTables` belong only to the consequence writer. The ledger alone inserts event evidence, rejection and disposition in one transaction; extend `$ledgerConflictAllowed` regex with exactly `ON CONFLICT (tenant_id,site_id,source_id,record_kind,event_id) WHERE family='EVENT' DO NOTHING`, matching the partial unique index. No `DO UPDATE` permission is added. The race loser rereads the accepted ID and chooses duplicate/event conflict by canonical SHA before writing rejection/disposition.

Set `$ledgerConflictAllowed` to the double-quoted PowerShell regex `"(?i)\bON\s+CONFLICT\s+(?:ON\s+CONSTRAINT\s+(?:batch_refusal_identity|ingestion_batch_external_unique|accepted_evidence_message_unique)|\(tenant_id,\s*site_id,\s*source_id,\s*record_kind,\s*event_id\)\s+WHERE\s+family\s*=\s*'EVENT')\s+DO\s+NOTHING\b"`; update its error text from â€œthreeâ€ to â€œfourâ€. `ledger_reads.py` uses `kind IN ('MESSAGE_CONFLICT','EVENT_ID_CONFLICT','SESSION_ID_CONFLICT')` in both receipt count locations (lines 232 and 376), retaining existing `MESSAGE_CONFLICT` objects and bytes. No migration touches old Receipt V2 rows.

Cold money work is triggered by an appended cold FindingRevision, appended cold episode revision, relevant assumption publication or source-gate change; a migration trigger inserts a dirty `(site,window,domain='cold_chain')` work row with unique dirty key and `DO NOTHING` duplicate suppression. The consequence worker leases by `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, selects the exact settled assessment/Finding basis, writes immutable consequence/evidence rows through `postgres_consequence_store.py`, and marks the dirty row done in the same transaction. Migration trigger is the sole work-row inserter. `tools/writer_guard_probes.py` gains pass probes for each named owner/table, permitted work UPDATE and partial-index conflict target, and fail probes for runtime work INSERT, foreign writer, immutable UPDATE/DELETE, `DO UPDATE`, and an unlisted conflict arbiter. `configuration-persistence.ps1` must count these probes and fail closed when an owner is absent. `overlap_group` is SHA-256 of canonical `(tenant_id,site_id,window_start,window_end,room_asset_id)` prefixed `cold:`; excursion and backup consequences for that room/window share it.

### Response vocabulary

Every object includes `as_of: UTC timestamp|null` and `limitation: string|null`. Common `currentness.state` is exactly `CURRENT|PENDING_RECOMPUTE|STALE_SOURCE|HISTORICAL|UNKNOWN`; `source_provenance` is `{classification: ATTESTED_REAL_SOURCE|REGISTERED_SIMULATION|AUTHORED|MIXED|UNKNOWN, source_ids: string[], evidence_ids: UUID[], basis_sha256: string|null}`; root `limitations` is `string[]`. `start_bounds,end_bounds,episode_bounds` are `{earliest: UTC timestamp|null,latest: UTC timestamp|null}`; `duration_bounds,degree_minute_bounds,quantity_bounds,amount_bounds` are `{low: decimal|null,high: decimal|null}`. `gaps` is `[{start:UTC timestamp,end:UTC timestamp,reason:MISSING_PROBE|SUSPECT|CLOCK_UNATTESTED|CONTRADICTORY|UNBOUND,expected_points:int,received_points:int}]`, with half-open bounds. `coverage` is a decimal in `[0,1]` plus `{known_points:int,expected_points:int}` on temperature/evidence cards.

## 8. Slice fixtures, replay and vendor peer

### Writer, trigger and transaction contract

The 0022 generalization is mandatory before 0025. For `ev_charging`, `analytic_run.cohort_status='NOT_APPLICABLE'`, `cohort=[]`, `cohort_sha256=sha256(canonical([]))`; the complete Finding/Revision NOT NULL mapping and `'mini_grid'` keyed CHECKs in the cold design apply. EV FindingRevision `episode_start=START source_time`, `episode_end=ABORT source_time`; its three dispatch minute columns are null. `support_sha256` hashes sorted exact START/FAULT/ABORT evidence ID+SHA pairs, `cited_evidence_sha256` hashes the exact cited accepted set, and `input_evidence_sha256=analytic_run.input_sha256`. No fault-abort Finding is created before all three accepted facts exist.

Rule 8 `tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1`: add `ev_session,ev_session_revision,ev_session_evidence,ev_meter_interval` to `$assessTables` before `$assessInserts` is derived; add `ev_consequence_revision,ev_consequence_session,ev_consequence_evidence` to `$consequenceTables`, and `ev_consequence_work` to `$consequenceWorkTables`. `postgres_assessment_store.py` exclusively inserts the four session tables, `postgres_finding_store.py` exclusively inserts Findings, and `postgres_consequence_store.py` exclusively inserts the three EV consequence tables and leases/completes work. The consequence writer branch allows `UPDATE ev_consequence_work SET status=...,lease_owner=...,lease_until=...,updated_at=... WHERE id=...` with only those four assigned columns, and rejects runtime work INSERT, other UPDATE and DELETE. Cross-writer checks cover `$consequenceTables + $consequenceWorkTables`. `assessment_worker.py` owns one caller-supplied transaction containing analytic run, assessment, session revision/links, FindingRevision/links and job completion. `consequence_worker.py` owns a separate transaction containing consequence/links and work completion. No SQL writer enters HTTP/domain modules and no `DO UPDATE` allowlist entry is added. The 0020 ledger event-conflict target/allowlist and Receipt V2 classification are inherited unchanged. `tools/writer_guard_probes.py` adds permitted owner INSERT and work UPDATE, rejects runtime work INSERT, foreign owner, immutable UPDATE/DELETE, unlisted `ON CONFLICT` and dynamic SQL; it includes pass/fail probes for both multi-adapter transaction paths. The guard fails when a named module/table is missing.

`0026` adds `ev_consequence_work` (`id uuid PK`, tenant/site, window_start/end, dirty_basis_sha256, status `PENDING|LEASED|DONE|FAILED`, lease_owner/lease_until nullable, created_at/updated_at; UNIQUE `(site_id,window_start,window_end,dirty_basis_sha256)`). Migration triggers insert dirty work on appended EV session/assessment revision, accepted late EV meter/event, tariff publication, and source-gate revision. Duplicate dirtiness uses `DO NOTHING` on the unique key inside the trigger, not a runtime writer. `postgres_consequence_store.py` leases with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, writes a Site window revision and links, and marks work done atomically. `ev_consequence_revision.revision_no` is `1+max(revision_no)` for the locked `(tenant,site,window_start,window_end)` lineage, guarded by the existing unique `(site_id,window_start,window_end,revision_no)` and retried on collision. `overlap_group='ev:'+sha256(canonical(tenant_id,site_id,window_start,window_end,tariff_revision_id))`, using null tariff ID text `NO_TARIFF` for unpriced revisions.

### Response vocabulary

Use the cold design's common `currentness.state`, `source_provenance`, `limitations`, UUID/time/decimal JSON types and exact envelope. Session `reason_codes` is an array drawn only from `MISSING_START|MISSING_TERMINAL|CONFLICTING_TERMINAL|CONFLICTING_START|CONNECTOR_MISMATCH|METER_GAP|METER_OVERLAP|METER_INVALID|CLOCK_UNATTESTED|SOURCE_UNVERIFIED|PENDING_RECOMPUTE`. `links` is `{self:string|null,evidence:string|null,analysis:string|null,finding:string|null,financials:string|null}`; null means no supported destination. Foundation `readiness.state=READY_TO_REPORT|CONFIGURATION_INCOMPLETE`, `commissioning.state=CONFIRMED|UNCONFIRMED`, `contact.state=RECENT|STALE|NEVER`; `messages` uses exactly the codes in Â§2. 35: ## 3. W2-05b versioned charger/session producer
36: 
37: Add `ev-charger/1` `ModelSpec`, `ev-publication/1`, `ev-observation/1` to the part-1 closed host registry, preserving all existing model identities (`host/execution_adapter.py:123-149`; `.agent/W2-technical-design-cold.md:59-69`). Reject unknown pack/profile/parameter version before Draft. Frozen run identity includes Foundation/binding/source IDs, scenario SHA, pack/profile/observation versions, UTC window and seed. Stage seals only public v4 reports; registered Commit â†’ SQLite outbox â†’ authenticated Batch V2 â†’ S2 accepted evidence. No direct evidence/Finding seed, Lab private state, vehicle/customer identity or hidden demand in envelopes (`host/lab_execution.py:2154-2164,2428-2501`; `frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md:131,183`).
38: 
39: **Discrete model.** One-minute UTC step, exact decimal arithmetic; default fixture has two 60 kW connectors, 80 kW site import cap, both enabled 08:00â€“16:00 UTC on 2026-10-06, site non-EV base load 0 kW. State per connector: `UNPLUGGED|PLUGGED|AUTHORIZING|CHARGING|FAULTED`, session ID or null, authorization result, requested kW, allocated kW, meter accumulator kWh, fault latch. Private forcing: plug/unplug, authorization accept/deny, request schedule, fault/clear, grid interruption, publication dropout/latency. Initial unplugged, no session/fault, accumulator 0. At each minute: apply forcing ordered `(time,connector,kind)`; a plug assigns next deterministic scoped session ID; authorization takes 1 minute; acceptance emits START when CHARGING begins; denial produces no START and is a contextual observed state, not a failed session. Allocate shared available power `max(0,site_limit-base_load)` by connector ID ascending in round-robin 1 kW quanta among charging connectors up to `min(request,connector_limit,charger_limit)`; persist round-robin pointer so no connector monopolizes successive minutes. `E_next=E+allocated_kW/60`, exact kWh. A fault latches FAULTED, emits FAULT and ABORT for an active started session; explicit user unplug emits END (completed) only after active charge and no fault; grid interruption pauses allocation and connector state, it does not invent ABORT. Fault clear requires explicit forcing and emits CLEAR; a new plug creates a new session. Simultaneous END/ABORT or START twice for one ID is prohibited by simulator sanity guard. Fail run on negative energy, allocated sum above available/site/connector limits, energy residual, state with no valid session, duplicate conflicting forcing or non-monotone time.
40: 
41: **Public transform.** `ev-publication/1`: connector state at transitions and every minute; connector interval energy delta over aligned five-minute `[start,end)` slots (including zero while an active accepted session pauses), with session ID in the W2-00 v4 measurement payload; power mean per slot and site import mean per slot; session/fault events at observed transitions. Every v4 measurement contains `session_id,connector_id`; both are nonnull for connector energy and explicit null on cold/site channels. Connector ID must equal the bound EV_CONNECTOR asset; S2 refuses `EV_CONNECTOR_MISMATCH`. `source_time=interval_end` for interval reports. Split a session boundary inside a slot into nonoverlapping subintervals. Round accumulator to 0.001 kWh half-even at both ends and difference rounded values, preserving additive deltas; meter bound 0.001 kWh per boundary. Event IDs derive from scenario SHA, channel and transition ordinal; retransmission preserves IDs/bytes. Observation gap drops a channel report, never changes the world; delayed report retains source time and message identity. Mapping `simulator-lab-gateway-ev/1` exactly allows `(charger-controller,session-event)`, `(connector-controller,fault-event)`, `(connector-controller,state)`, `(connector-meter,energy)`, `(connector-meter,power)`, `(site-meter,import)`, `(connector-controller,requested-power)`, `(connector-controller,allocated-limit)` with listed kinds/units/cadences (`simulator/assetops_simulator/gateway/mappings.py:22-46,87-107`).

54: ## 4. W2-06 session correlation, meter sum, Finding, currentness
55: 
56: Select only accepted, GOOD, clock-attested records with valid effective Foundation/binding and exact `(tenant,site,charger,connector,session_id)`; EVENT session ID comes from W2-00, energy identity from the v4 measurement extension above. Require one START, at most one terminal END or ABORT, ordered by source time with <=30-second declared cross-source clock error; a terminal without START remains orphan context, not a session. An END is COMPLETED only when same scoped ID, no competing ABORT, and no contradictory state; explicit ABORT proves OBSERVED_ABORT, while contemporaneous fault on the same connector/session is additionally required for a **fault-associated** Finding. Fault alone does not prove an abort. Missing terminal is INCOMPLETE regardless of apparently idle status; conflicting good terminal/start times or incompatible connector identity is CONTRADICTORY. `SUSPECT` and unbound reports are context/excluded with reasons, never settle outcomes. Repeat events are deduped by W2-00 and cross-tenant/session collisions cannot join.
57: 
58: For each session, intersect GOOD connector `INTERVAL_DELTA` reports with `[start,terminal)` and same session ID; accept only wholly contained, nonoverlapping half-open intervals with finite `0<=kWh<=connector_max_kW*duration_hours+2*meter_error_bound`, and matching exact source. Do **not** pro-rate a report spanning a boundary, choose between conflicting duplicates, substitute power integration or infer zero for an absent slot. `supported_kwh=sum(exact accepted deltas)`; `unknown_meter_seconds` is the union duration of uncovered active 5-minute slots, clipped to session bounds. A delivered-energy total is **complete** only if every active slot is covered (partial boundary slots must have a split report) and no contradiction; otherwise report known partial sum with `energy_complete=false` and unknown time, never call it total delivered. If no supported intervals, quantity null rather than zero. Session outcome counts use START denominator, separately completed/observed abort/incomplete/contradictory; orphan records are separate. No availability percentage or site constraint cause in W2. Synchronized site import below limit is context only; a power-constraint claim requires independent accepted requested and allocated-limit signals plus site import over the same slots, and W2 still labels competing causes unknown (`.agent/architect-W2-design.md:37-49`).
59: 
60: `ev-session/1` assessment basis SHA sorts accepted evidence IDs+content digests, excluded/context IDs+reasons, effective Foundation/binding/mapping IDs, model/rule versions, `[from,to)`, `known_at` and source classification. Session revision and its evidence/interval links append atomically with `assessment_revision` and any FindingRevision. A fault-abort Finding uses the stable key above, with exact fault/abort/START evidence and limitation â€œfault observed; component cause and unmet demand unknown.â€ A completed late END supersedes prior INCOMPLETE by new revision; a contradictory terminal withdraws previous current conclusion. Worker enqueue on **every** newly accepted relevant EV report, including late source time; until latest applicable job settles, previous projection reads `PENDING_RECOMPUTE`, with historical exact revision separately selectable. For current status, latest needed connector state <=2 minutes old at read time and terminal/event sources within declared cadence; old closed sessions may remain historical conclusions. Current service-window coverage uses one-minute status slots and explicit `known_seconds/unknown_seconds`; no available-time inference over gaps >2 minutes. Source gate follows S4-05 exact revision provenance; generated and authored evidence are labelled and mixed/unknown blocks a current positive (`.ai/DECISIONS.md:5-15,4165-4188`).

62: ## 5. W2-07 tariff scenario and claim gate
63: 
64: `ev_gross_tariff_per_kwh` is a Site time-valid, recorded revision in `currency/kWh`, with authored basis/source; no default and no billing/collection identity. A window may price only supported energy in completed or observed-abort sessions, each with complete meter coverage and settled current assessment; no session interval is counted twice. For every included meter interval, select exactly one tariff revision whose **valid** range covers it and whose **recorded** range is visible at `known_at`; W2 v1 requires the same revision and currency across the entire priced window, otherwise `UNPRICED/PRICE_GAP_OR_CHANGE` (no averaging). Formula `amount = Î£ exact interval kWh Ã— tariff value`, unrounded in storage; display rounding only. `quantity_kwh` and exact session revision/accepted meter evidence/tariff revision IDs are pinned. No lost-sales estimate: private requested power, incomplete sessions and fault downtime do not measure counterfactual demand. An aborted session can contribute **delivered** pre-abort energy, never lost energy. W1 LR current-money gate and S4-05 coherent source gate apply: pending/late/revised assessment, conflicting meter, historical cutoff, mixed/unknown provenance, unsupported tariff, or missing price â†’ `WITHHELD` or `UNPRICED`, null amount (not zero). Historical amount may appear labelled historical only under its exact old basis, never as today's current money. `overlap_group='ev:'+sha256(canonical(tenant_id,site_id,window_start,window_end,tariff_revision_id))` prevents adding the same meter intervals through multiple Findings; Site scenario is one window revision, not per-Finding additive totals (`backend/migrations/versions/0010_financial_assumptions.py:38-102`; `.ai/DECISIONS.md:5-15`).

66: ## 6. Selected reads and refusals
67: 
68: All routes are tenant-authenticated Site-code reads. Except Foundation/replay, require UTC half-open `from,to`, max 31 days, optional UTC `known_at` (default latest committed watermark); a historical cutoff is labelled historical. Common response `{site_id,window:{from,to,known_at},currentness:{state,watermark,latest_relevant_evidence_at,pending_since},source_provenance,limitations,...}`. Watermark pins latest included S2 decision time + assessment revision; lists use opaque `(time,id)` cursor, default limit 50/max 200, `{items,next_cursor}`. Shared error `{error:{code,message}}`: 401 unauthenticated, 403 wrong tenant/Lab gate/source-gated detail, 404 missing/wrong Site type or record, 409 unresolved selected revision or conflicting current recompute, 422 bad parameter/window/selector, 503 ledger/projection unavailable. Valid evidence gaps return 200 with UNKNOWN/null and reasons. No endpoint returns private forcing/oracle, customer/vehicle or a fabricated zero (`.agent/W2-technical-design-cold.md:83-107`; `backend/assetops_backend/evidence_api.py:54-59,157-161`).

99: ## 8. Slice fixtures, replay and vendor peer
100: 
101: ### Writer, trigger and transaction contract
102: 
103: The 0022 generalization is mandatory before 0025. For `ev_charging`, `analytic_run.cohort_status='NOT_APPLICABLE'`, `cohort=[]`, `cohort_sha256=sha256(canonical([]))`; the complete Finding/Revision NOT NULL mapping and `'mini_grid'` keyed CHECKs in the cold design apply. EV FindingRevision `episode_start=START source_time`, `episode_end=ABORT source_time`; its three dispatch minute columns are null. `support_sha256` hashes sorted exact START/FAULT/ABORT evidence ID+SHA pairs, `cited_evidence_sha256` hashes the exact cited accepted set, and `input_evidence_sha256=analytic_run.input_sha256`. No fault-abort Finding is created before all three accepted facts exist.
104: 
105: Rule 8 `tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1`: add `ev_session,ev_session_revision,ev_session_evidence,ev_meter_interval` to `$assessTables` before `$assessInserts` is derived; add `ev_consequence_revision,ev_consequence_session,ev_consequence_evidence` to `$consequenceTables`, and `ev_consequence_work` to `$consequenceWorkTables`. `postgres_assessment_store.py` exclusively inserts the four session tables, `postgres_finding_store.py` exclusively inserts Findings, and `postgres_consequence_store.py` exclusively inserts the three EV consequence tables and leases/completes work. The consequence writer branch allows `UPDATE ev_consequence_work SET status=...,lease_owner=...,lease_until=...,updated_at=... WHERE id=...` with only those four assigned columns, and rejects runtime work INSERT, other UPDATE and DELETE. Cross-writer checks cover `$consequenceTables + $consequenceWorkTables`. `assessment_worker.py` owns one caller-supplied transaction containing analytic run, assessment, session revision/links, FindingRevision/links and job completion. `consequence_worker.py` owns a separate transaction containing consequence/links and work completion. No SQL writer enters HTTP/domain modules and no `DO UPDATE` allowlist entry is added. The 0020 ledger event-conflict target/allowlist and Receipt V2 classification are inherited unchanged. `tools/writer_guard_probes.py` adds permitted owner INSERT and work UPDATE, rejects runtime work INSERT, foreign owner, immutable UPDATE/DELETE, unlisted `ON CONFLICT` and dynamic SQL; it includes pass/fail probes for both multi-adapter transaction paths. The guard fails when a named module/table is missing.
106: 
107: `0026` adds `ev_consequence_work` (`id uuid PK`, tenant/site, window_start/end, dirty_basis_sha256, status `PENDING|LEASED|DONE|FAILED`, lease_owner/lease_until nullable, created_at/updated_at; UNIQUE `(site_id,window_start,window_end,dirty_basis_sha256)`). Migration triggers insert dirty work on appended EV session/assessment revision, accepted late EV meter/event, tariff publication, and source-gate revision. Duplicate dirtiness uses `DO NOTHING` on the unique key inside the trigger, not a runtime writer. `postgres_consequence_store.py` leases with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, writes a Site window revision and links, and marks work done atomically. `ev_consequence_revision.revision_no` is `1+max(revision_no)` for the locked `(tenant,site,window_start,window_end)` lineage, guarded by the existing unique `(site_id,window_start,window_end,revision_no)` and retried on collision. `overlap_group='ev:'+sha256(canonical(tenant_id,site_id,window_start,window_end,tariff_revision_id))`, using null tariff ID text `NO_TARIFF` for unpriced revisions.

Foundation `readiness.state=READY_TO_REPORT|CONFIGURATION_INCOMPLETE`, `commissioning.state=CONFIRMED|UNCONFIRMED`, `contact.state=RECENT|STALE|NEVER`; `messages` uses exactly the codes in Â§2.  succeeded in 243ms:
76: Grid loss opens transfer and deenergizes compressor bus; controller emits `START_REQUEST` only if backup enabled, generator reaches RUNNING after delay unless failed, transfer closes to BACKUP after delay if generator power exists, and compressor restarts after its own delay if controller still demands cooling. Failed start emits `START_RESULT/FAILED` at timeout; successful start emits `START_RESULT/SUCCEEDED` when running. Transfer result is emitted only if a position sensor is configured; absence stays absence. Door events are sensor transitions; door opening adds heat only while open. Probe failure emits one `UNAVAILABLE` reading with reason `SENSOR_FAILURE` if the profile publishes it, then no numeric sample; reporting-path loss emits no envelope. Device measurement is `T + fixed observation bias` (default 0), rounded to 0.1Â°C by half-even rule; no private cause field. Controller and power events use observed public transitions and stable correlation ID.

Assets are `room-1`, `probe-1`, `door-1`, `generator-1`, `grid-1`, `bus-1`, `compressor-1`; generator backup enabled. Base initial state is T=3.00Â°C, ambient=20Â°C, door CLOSED, compressor OFF, grid/bus POWERED, generator STOPPED, transfer GRID, probe OK; excursion-derived cases override initial T to 3.80Â°C. Each row lists the entire non-default forcing, ordered by `(time,kind,asset)`; observation faults occur after a valid world transition and cannot bypass `PhysicallyUnacceptable` guards.
