89: `cold-publication/1` emits chamber `STATE_AT_INSTANT degC` every 5 minutes including end; grid/generator/compressor/bus states on transitions and 5-minute heartbeat; compressor `INTERVAL_MEAN kW` per aligned 5-minute slot; door/start/transfer/power events on transition. `cold-observation/1` freezes the three specified hold/drop/fault variants, zero bias and default 3-second latency before Draft. The model retains one calibrated primary chamber probe per room; delete the two-primary-probe disagreement rule. Contradictory distinct GOOD controller outcomes for one attempt cause `CONTRADICTORY`. `cold-door-heat` property unit is `kWh/min`, with 0.02 per open minute; the equation's `*60` converts it to kW before its one-minute integration. The Lab persisted-state validator uses the `cold-thermal/1` finite vocabularies above, not the mini-grid generator lifecycle vocabulary; extend the closed identity/profile registries. `Role.value_kind` adds `EVENT`, and EVENT bindings use `conversion='IDENTITY'`, with typed outcome allowlist and no numeric conversion. Foundation controls stay NUMBER, not a new `Role.value_kind`.
90: 
91: The closed cold mapping uses `(device_id,signal_id)`: `(chamber-probe,temperature)` degC point, `(ambient-probe,temperature)` degC point, `(grid-meter,state)` state point, `(generator-controller,state)` state point, `(compressor-controller,state)` state point, `(backup-bus-meter,state)` state point, `(compressor-meter,power)` kW interval mean, `(door-sensor,event)` door events, `(generator-controller,start-event)` request/result events, `(transfer-controller,event)` transfer result, `(grid-meter,event)` loss/restored events, `(operator-log,temperature-event)` manual log. Each tuple is a separate versioned mapping entry with only its listed kinds, values and cadence. W2-00 also reserves EV entries `(charger-controller,session-event)` for session start/end/abort and `(connector-controller,fault-event)` for fault/clear, with connector/session identity rules above; W2-05b supplies the EV pack and its own frozen mapping version. The same signal ID on a different device is not an implicit alias (`simulator/assetops_simulator/gateway/mappings.py:22-46,87-107`).
92: 
93: ## 5. W2-03 assessment, episodes and Findings
94: 
95: Analytic IDs `cold-excursion/1`, `cold-start-response/1`. Input selection is accepted evidence only, fixed half-open `[from,to)`, explicit `known_at` cutoff on server `received_at`/decision time, effective Foundation/binding versions, and source gate. Include exact accepted IDs and SHA, configuration IDs, model version, window/cutoff in sorted basis SHA. A reading is usable only if GOOD, calibrated, mapped to the room, and clock-attested; SUSPECT is context. Expected five-minute points: coverage = usable distinct scheduled timestamps / scheduled timestamps, capped at 1; `>=0.8` needed for an interval claim, but no gap >10 minutes may be bridged. Latest point must be <=10 minutes old at read time for a **current** state; historical window conclusion can remain bounded with explicit as-of. A failed worker or newer accepted relevant evidence marks prior projection `PENDING_RECOMPUTE` until a new immutable revision is written.
96: 
97: Excursion begins after two consecutive GOOD observations above upper band (or below lower band) at least 5 minutes apart; return requires two consecutive observations inside band by at least 0.2Â°C. The start is bounded by `[last in-band, first out-of-band]`; return by `[last out-of-band, first stable in-band]`; duration low = max(0, earliest return - latest start), high = latest return - earliest start, computed only across continuously covered spans. Initial out-of-band sample with no prior in-band gives unknown start; window end without return gives open episode and no finite high. `degree_minutes` bounds use observed rectangles only between adjacent usable samples <=10 minutes apart: low uses min adjacent exceedance, high max adjacent exceedance times interval minutes; add no unobserved gap area. Peak is the maximum **observed** value. An exposure Finding requires 15-minute lower duration and coverage >=0.8; otherwise `POSSIBLE` or `UNKNOWN`, never â€œno excursionâ€ from missing samples. In-band fully covered is `NO_EXCURSION_OBSERVED` scoped to window, not equipment health.
98: 
99: Start chain groups by accepted `correlation_id`, generator asset and outage window. Require ordered grid LOST â†’ START_REQUEST â†’ START_RESULT â†’ generator RUNNING/FAILED â†’ optional TRANSFER_RESULT â†’ bus POWERED/UNPOWERED â†’ compressor state â†’ probe response; tolerance is source-clock bound <=30 seconds for event ordering, with timeout from effective Foundation (default 5 minutes). A failed automatic start is `OBSERVED_FAILURE` only with request, explicit failed result or timeout plus complete generator state coverage through timeout, and no contradictory success; missing request/result/state is `UNKNOWN_STEP`. A transfer-position claim requires accepted transfer event; bus energization alone proves only bus state. Cooling failure cannot be assigned from temperature alone. Emit claim levels `OBSERVED`, `BOUNDED`, `POSSIBLE`, `UNKNOWN`, `CONTRADICTORY`; explanations list exact missing/excluded records and claim ceiling.
100: 
101: Episode key = SHA-256 of `(tenant,site,kind,subject room/generator asset,first accepted qualifying crossing/request event ID)`; retain anchor when late evidence adjusts boundaries, append `cold_episode_revision` and corresponding FindingRevision with transition `SUPPORT_CHANGED` or `BASIS_REFRESHED`. Two episodes split only after two stable in-band points (excursion) or a new start request (backup). If late evidence invalidates the anchor, append `NO_LONGER_SUPPORTED` and create a new episode with `split_from` relation; never rewrite old. Separate excursion and start-response Findings can cite the same event but share one `overlap_group` for money. Use existing S4-05 exact accepted-ID provenance and source classification (`backend/migrations/versions/0009_finding_store.py:33-87`; `.ai/DECISIONS.md:5-15`).
102: 
103: ## 6. W2-04 consequence, reads and UI
104: 
105: `PRODUCT_EXPOSURE` is a bounded thermal exposure indicator in `degC_min`, **not product quantity, spoilage or loss**. `cold_stock_quantity`, `cold_stock_unit_value` (currency/unit) and a product-specific reviewed `cold_exposure_rule` would be needed for a future valued risk scenario; W2 creates no default and still forbids an amount without an approved rule. `RECORDED_REPAIR` amount requires exact accepted work/cost record, currency, time and non-overlap; no such source exists in W2, so it is `UNPRICED`. All consequences pin FindingRevision, episode revision, accepted IDs, assumption revision IDs, model/formula version, source gate and overlap group. Missing/contradictory probe or policy, stale projection, mixed/unknown provenance, undeclared stock or cost â†’ `UNPRICED`/`WITHHELD` with null amount. Never sum backup and excursion exposure; no zero amount as stand-in for unknown. The existing LR current-money and S4-05 source gates still apply (`backend/migrations/versions/0010_financial_assumptions.py:1-30`; `backend/migrations/versions/0011_financial_consequences.py:43-69`; `.ai/DECISIONS.md:5-15`).
106: 
107: All routes below use Site code, authenticated tenant scope, UTC `from,to` (end exclusive), optional UTC `known_at` (default current committed watermark), max 31-day window, and return `{site_id,window:{from,to,known_at},currentness:{state,watermark,latest_relevant_evidence_at,pending_since},source_provenance,limitations,...}`. `watermark` is latest included ingestion disposition `decided_at` plus assessment revision ID; a selected historical cutoff cannot claim today's currentness. Lists use opaque `(time,id)` cursor, `limit` default 50 max 200 and `{items,next_cursor}`. Shared refusal envelope `{error:{code,message}}`: 401 unauthenticated, 403 wrong tenant/source-gated disclosure, 404 Site/record not found or wrong type, 409 unresolved selected revision or recompute conflict, 422 malformed/window/range/unknown parameter, 503 ledger/projection unavailable; a valid missing claim returns 200 with `UNKNOWN`/null, never 404 or a fabricated zero. Existing evidence/Finding paths continue to serve exact links (`backend/assetops_backend/evidence_api.py:157-161`; `backend/assetops_backend/finding_api.py:102-189`).

130: ## 7. Slice proof fixtures and acceptance
131: 
132: ### Writer guard and money trigger contract
133: 
134: Rule 8 in `tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1` is extended exactly: add `cold_episode,cold_episode_revision,cold_episode_evidence` to `$assessTables` before `$assessInserts` is derived; add `cold_consequence_revision,cold_consequence_evidence` to `$consequenceTables`; add `cold_consequence_work` to a new `$consequenceWorkTables` array. `postgres_assessment_store.py` alone inserts episode and analytic/assessment rows; `postgres_finding_store.py` alone inserts Findings. `assessment_worker.py` owns one caller-supplied transaction spanning both adapters and job completion, commits after both have inserted, and retries the whole transaction on serializable conflict. `postgres_consequence_store.py` alone inserts cold consequence rows and leases/completes work. In the consequence writer branch, allow `INSERT` only into `$consequenceTables`, and `UPDATE cold_consequence_work SET status=...,lease_owner=...,lease_until=...,updated_at=... WHERE id=...` with assigned columns limited to those four; reject runtime `INSERT` into work and all other UPDATE/DELETE. In the cross-writer branch, `$consequenceTables + $consequenceWorkTables` belong only to the consequence writer. The ledger alone inserts event evidence, rejection and disposition in one transaction; extend `$ledgerConflictAllowed` regex with exactly `ON CONFLICT (tenant_id,site_id,source_id,record_kind,event_id) WHERE family='EVENT' DO NOTHING`, matching the partial unique index. No `DO UPDATE` permission is added. The race loser rereads the accepted ID and chooses duplicate/event conflict by canonical SHA before writing rejection/disposition.
135: 
136: Set `$ledgerConflictAllowed` to the double-quoted PowerShell regex `"(?i)\bON\s+CONFLICT\s+(?:ON\s+CONSTRAINT\s+(?:batch_refusal_identity|ingestion_batch_external_unique|accepted_evidence_message_unique)|\(tenant_id,\s*site_id,\s*source_id,\s*record_kind,\s*event_id\)\s+WHERE\s+family\s*=\s*'EVENT')\s+DO\s+NOTHING\b"`; update its error text from â€œthreeâ€ to â€œfourâ€. `ledger_reads.py` uses `kind IN ('MESSAGE_CONFLICT','EVENT_ID_CONFLICT','SESSION_ID_CONFLICT')` in both receipt count locations (lines 232 and 376), retaining existing `MESSAGE_CONFLICT` objects and bytes. No migration touches old Receipt V2 rows.
137: 
138: Cold money work is triggered by an appended cold FindingRevision, appended cold episode revision, relevant assumption publication or source-gate change; a migration trigger inserts a dirty `(site,window,domain='cold_chain')` work row with unique dirty key and `DO NOTHING` duplicate suppression. The consequence worker leases by `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, selects the exact settled assessment/Finding basis, writes immutable consequence/evidence rows through `postgres_consequence_store.py`, and marks the dirty row done in the same transaction. Migration trigger is the sole work-row inserter. `tools/writer_guard_probes.py` gains pass probes for each named owner/table, permitted work UPDATE and partial-index conflict target, and fail probes for runtime work INSERT, foreign writer, immutable UPDATE/DELETE, `DO UPDATE`, and an unlisted conflict arbiter. `configuration-persistence.ps1` must count these probes and fail closed when an owner is absent. `overlap_group` is SHA-256 of canonical `(tenant_id,site_id,window_start,window_end,room_asset_id)` prefixed `cold:`; excursion and backup consequences for that room/window share it.

140: ### Response vocabulary
141: 
142: Every object includes `as_of: UTC timestamp|null` and `limitation: string|null`. Common `currentness.state` is exactly `CURRENT|PENDING_RECOMPUTE|STALE_SOURCE|HISTORICAL|UNKNOWN`; `source_provenance` is `{classification: ATTESTED_REAL_SOURCE|REGISTERED_SIMULATION|AUTHORED|MIXED|UNKNOWN, source_ids: string[], evidence_ids: UUID[], basis_sha256: string|null}`; root `limitations` is `string[]`. `start_bounds,end_bounds,episode_bounds` are `{earliest: UTC timestamp|null,latest: UTC timestamp|null}`; `duration_bounds,degree_minute_bounds,quantity_bounds,amount_bounds` are `{low: decimal|null,high: decimal|null}`. `gaps` is `[{start:UTC timestamp,end:UTC timestamp,reason:MISSING_PROBE|SUSPECT|CLOCK_UNATTESTED|CONTRADICTORY|UNBOUND,expected_points:int,received_points:int}]`, with half-open bounds. `coverage` is a decimal in `[0,1]` plus `{known_points:int,expected_points:int}` on temperature/evidence cards.

The ledger alone inserts event evidence, rejection and disposition in one transaction; extend `$ledgerConflictAllowed` regex with exactly `ON CONFLICT (tenant_id,site_id,source_id,record_kind,event_id) WHERE family='EVENT' DO NOTHING`, matching the partial unique index. No `DO UPDATE` permission is added. The race loser rereads the accepted ID and chooses duplicate/event conflict by canonical SHA before writing rejection/disposition.
135: 
136: Set `$ledgerConflictAllowed` to the double-quoted PowerShell regex `"(?i)\bON\s+CONFLICT\s+(?:ON\s+CONSTRAINT\s+(?:batch_refusal_identity|ingestion_batch_external_unique|accepted_evidence_message_unique)|\(tenant_id,\s*site_id,\s*source_id,\s*record_kind,\s*event_id\)\s+WHERE\s+family\s*=\s*'EVENT')\s+DO\s+NOTHING\b"`; update its error text from â€œthreeâ€ to â€œfourâ€. `ledger_reads.py` uses `kind IN ('MESSAGE_CONFLICT','EVENT_ID_CONFLICT','SESSION_ID_CONFLICT')` in both receipt count locations (lines 232 and 376), retaining existing `MESSAGE_CONFLICT` objects and bytes. No migration touches old Receipt V2 rows.

138: Cold money work is triggered by an appended cold FindingRevision, appended cold episode revision, relevant assumption publication or source-gate change; a migration trigger inserts a dirty `(site,window,domain='cold_chain')` work row with unique dirty key and `DO NOTHING` duplicate suppression. The consequence worker leases by `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, selects the exact settled assessment/Finding basis, writes immutable consequence/evidence rows through `postgres_consequence_store.py`, and marks the dirty row done in the same transaction. Migration trigger is the sole work-row inserter. `tools/writer_guard_probes.py` gains pass probes for each named owner/table, permitted work UPDATE and partial-index conflict target, and fail probes for runtime work INSERT, foreign writer, immutable UPDATE/DELETE, `DO UPDATE`, and an unlisted conflict arbiter. `configuration-persistence.ps1` must count these probes and fail closed when an owner is absent. `overlap_group` is SHA-256 of canonical `(tenant_id,site_id,window_start,window_end,room_asset_id)` prefixed `cold:`; excursion and backup consequences for that room/window share it.

35: ## 3. W2-05b versioned charger/session producer
36: 
37: Add `ev-charger/1` `ModelSpec`, `ev-publication/1`, `ev-observation/1` to the part-1 closed host registry, preserving all existing model identities (`host/execution_adapter.py:123-149`; `.agent/W2-technical-design-cold.md:59-69`). Reject unknown pack/profile/parameter version before Draft. Frozen run identity includes Foundation/binding/source IDs, scenario SHA, pack/profile/observation versions, UTC window and seed. Stage seals only public v4 reports; registered Commit â†’ SQLite outbox â†’ authenticated Batch V2 â†’ S2 accepted evidence. No direct evidence/Finding seed, Lab private state, vehicle/customer identity or hidden demand in envelopes (`host/lab_execution.py:2154-2164,2428-2501`; `frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md:131,183`).
38: 
39: **Discrete model.** One-minute UTC step, exact decimal arithmetic; default fixture has two 60 kW connectors, 80 kW site import cap, both enabled 08:00â€“16:00 UTC on 2026-10-06, site non-EV base load 0 kW. State per connector: `UNPLUGGED|PLUGGED|AUTHORIZING|CHARGING|FAULTED`, session ID or null, authorization result, requested kW, allocated kW, meter accumulator kWh, fault latch. Private forcing: plug/unplug, authorization accept/deny, request schedule, fault/clear, grid interruption, publication dropout/latency. Initial unplugged, no session/fault, accumulator 0. At each minute: apply forcing ordered `(time,connector,kind)`; a plug assigns next deterministic scoped session ID; authorization takes 1 minute; acceptance emits START when CHARGING begins; denial produces no START and is a contextual observed state, not a failed session. Allocate shared available power `max(0,site_limit-base_load)` by connector ID ascending in round-robin 1 kW quanta among charging connectors up to `min(request,connector_limit,charger_limit)`; persist round-robin pointer so no connector monopolizes successive minutes. `E_next=E+allocated_kW/60`, exact kWh. A fault latches FAULTED, emits FAULT and ABORT for an active started session; explicit user unplug emits END (completed) only after active charge and no fault; grid interruption pauses allocation and connector state, it does not invent ABORT. Fault clear requires explicit forcing and emits CLEAR; a new plug creates a new session. Simultaneous END/ABORT or START twice for one ID is prohibited by simulator sanity guard. Fail run on negative energy, allocated sum above available/site/connector limits, energy residual, state with no valid session, duplicate conflicting forcing or non-monotone time.
40: 
41: **Public transform.** `ev-publication/1`: connector state at transitions and every minute; connector interval energy delta over aligned five-minute `[start,end)` slots (including zero while an active accepted session pauses), with session ID in the W2-00 v4 measurement payload; power mean per slot and site import mean per slot; session/fault events at observed transitions. Every v4 measurement contains `session_id,connector_id`; both are nonnull for connector energy and explicit null on cold/site channels. Connector ID must equal the bound EV_CONNECTOR asset; S2 refuses `EV_CONNECTOR_MISMATCH`. `source_time=interval_end` for interval reports. Split a session boundary inside a slot into nonoverlapping subintervals. Round accumulator to 0.001 kWh half-even at both ends and difference rounded values, preserving additive deltas; meter bound 0.001 kWh per boundary. Event IDs derive from scenario SHA, channel and transition ordinal; retransmission preserves IDs/bytes. Observation gap drops a channel report, never changes the world; delayed report retains source time and message identity. Mapping `simulator-lab-gateway-ev/1` exactly allows `(charger-controller,session-event)`, `(connector-controller,fault-event)`, `(connector-controller,state)`, `(connector-meter,energy)`, `(connector-meter,power)`, `(site-meter,import)`, `(connector-controller,requested-power)`, `(connector-controller,allocated-limit)` with listed kinds/units/cadences (`simulator/assetops_simulator/gateway/mappings.py:22-46,87-107`).

65: | `ambient_temperature` / `TEMPERATURE_PROBE` | point number `degC`, PER_ASSET | optional context, never substitutes chamber probe |
66: | `manual_temperature` / `COLD_ROOM` | EVENT manual log `degC`, PER_ASSET | optional context; never silently closes a probe gap |
67: 
68: Foundation topology uses explicit `COLD_ROOM`â†’`TEMPERATURE_PROBE`, `COMPRESSOR`â†’room, busâ†’compressor, generatorâ†’transferâ†’bus relations; every referenced asset must exist, one chamber probe cannot be primary for two rooms, and cycles/duplicate edges are refused. Policy band belongs to a room and is effective for the selected window; changing it creates a Foundation revision and binding set selection, never an in-place update. Validate finite exact positive thermal parameters, `0<=hysteresis<high-low`, probe error `>=0`, timeout `>0`, and cadence 5 minutes for the first Lab profile. Publication readiness must say **â€œAdd a chamber temperature probe and bind its Â°C readingâ€**, **â€œSet a lower and upper temperature policy for this roomâ€**, **â€œBind the generator start request and resultâ€**, or **â€œTransfer position is not reported; switch state is unknownâ€** as applicable. Configuration readiness, commissioning evidence and gateway contact remain separate (`backend/assetops_backend/sites/binding_service.py:267-317,674-715`; `.agent/architect-W2-design.md:32-53`).
69: 
70: ## 4. W2-02 causal producer and publication
71: 
72: Add `cold-thermal/1` `ModelSpec` and `cold-publication/1`; resolve frozen profile identity in `host/execution_adapter.py:113-140`, `host/lab_execution.py:1978-2015` and `simulator/assetops_simulator/kernel/execute.py:90-105` through a closed registry, with fuel/mini-grid identities preserved. Reject unknown pack/profile/parameter version before Draft. The kernel receives typed model laws and power ports, not a cold-specific branch. Lab freezes Foundation/binding IDs, scenario SHA, model/profile/observation versions and seed in run identity; Stage seals public v4 envelopes, registered Commit links publication, SQLite outbox sends authenticated Batch V2, and S2 alone creates accepted evidence (`host/lab_execution.py:2154-2164,2428-2501`; `.agent/architect-W2-design.md:55-66`).
73: 
74: **Time and state.** Fixed 1-minute UTC step; all updates use exact rationals/decimal text and a defined end-of-step publication order. State: chamber air temperature `T` degC, controller demand, compressor `OFF|STARTING|RUNNING|FAILED`, grid/backup bus energized flags, generator `STOPPED|STARTING|RUNNING|FAILED`, transfer position `GRID|BACKUP|OPEN`, door `OPEN|CLOSED`, probe `OK|FAILED`. Private forcing: ambient `Ta`, door schedule, grid loss, generator failure, probe failure; private truth never serializes into a product envelope. Initial `T=3Â°C`, `Ta=20Â°C`, door closed, compressor off, grid and bus on, generator stopped, transfer grid, probe OK. Per minute, `heat_kWh = (heat_leak_kW_per_C*(Ta-T) + door_open*door_heat_kWh*60)*1/60`; `cool_kWh = compressor_running_and_bus_powered*cooling_kW*1/60`; `T_next=T+(heat_kWh-cool_kWh)/thermal_capacity_kWh_per_C`. Defaults: thermal capacity `1.2 kWh/degC`, leak `0.08 kW/degC`, door heat `0.02 kWh/min` (stored as `1.2 kW` equivalent while open), cooling `3 kW`, compressor start delay 1 minute, generator start delay 2 minutes, transfer delay 1 minute, start timeout 5 minutes, control on `T>=4Â°C`, off `T<=2Â°C`, policy `[2,4]Â°C`, hysteresis 0.2Â°C, probe bound Â±0.1Â°C. Clamp no state: fail run if `T` outside `[-40,60]Â°C`, energy residual nonzero, negative capacity/power, or impossible transition. The standalone cold pack owns its generator and bus power port; W2-02 does not compose with mini-grid.
75: 
76: Grid loss opens transfer and deenergizes compressor bus; controller emits `START_REQUEST` only if backup enabled, generator reaches RUNNING after delay unless failed, transfer closes to BACKUP after delay if generator power exists, and compressor restarts after its own delay if controller still demands cooling. Failed start emits `START_RESULT/FAILED` at timeout; successful start emits `START_RESULT/SUCCEEDED` when running. Transfer result is emitted only if a position sensor is configured; absence stays absence. Door events are sensor transitions; door opening adds heat only while open. Probe failure emits one `UNAVAILABLE` reading with reason `SENSOR_FAILURE` if the profile publishes it, then no numeric sample; reporting-path loss emits no envelope. Device measurement is `T + fixed observation bias` (default 0), rounded to 0.1Â°C by half-even rule; no private cause field. Controller and power events use observed public transitions and stable correlation ID.

 `0022` adds nullable `finding.subject_asset_id uuid` with scoped asset FK; relaxes `finding.generator_asset_id` to nullable; adds CHECK `(domain='mini_grid' AND generator_asset_id IS NOT NULL) OR (domain='cold_chain' AND subject_asset_id IS NOT NULL AND generator_asset_id IS NULL)`. It also adds `finding_revision.domain text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'mini_grid'` for existing rows, drops the default after backfill, adds UNIQUE `(tenant_id,site_id,id,domain)` on `finding`, and replaces the revision's parent FK with `(tenant_id,site_id,finding_id,domain)` to that unique key. Thus revision CHECKs can use their own stored domain without trusting a caller. `0025` widens both domain CHECKs with `'ev_charging'`. One literal per domain is used in Finding, FindingRevision, `recompute_job.domain`, and episode/session producers: `mini_grid`, `cold_chain`, `ev_charging`. Existing `mini_grid` rows stay untouched apart from the additive revision domain backfill. `recompute_job.domain` has no SQL CHECK. `0022` adds the `(tenant_id,site_id,domain,subject_asset_id,anchor_start)` index and updates Finding store/read selection by domain. The shared fuel consequence CHECK remains intact. The `0020` accepted-event unique index is enforced in the ledger transaction; race losers get the conflict disposition above.
 
-For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. The ledger INSERT uses the event-identity partial conflict target; a unique violation on `accepted_ev_start_unique` rolls back that record's savepoint, rereads the first START, and emits `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT` in the same batch transaction. A unique violation on another constraint is not converted into this code. The session unique index is the race arbiter, not an application precheck.
+For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. Every accepted-evidence INSERT, including EVENT and EV START, retains `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT accepted_evidence_message_unique DO NOTHING`. For each ordinal, the ledger opens a savepoint around that INSERT. On a message-target `DO NOTHING` or a unique violation of `accepted_event_identity_unique` or `accepted_ev_start_unique`, it rolls back to the savepoint where needed and rereads under the same batch transaction in fixed order: message key, then event identity, then EV START identity. A matching message SHA returns `DUPLICATE`; a changed message SHA returns `MESSAGE_CONFLICT` even when the event or START key also collides. If the message key is free, a matching event SHA returns `DUPLICATE` and a changed event SHA returns `EVENT_ID_CONFLICT`; only if neither key matches does a distinct START collision return `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT`. Thus message wins a double collision, then event wins over session. Each conflict references the first accepted row for its winning key. A non-unique or unlisted unique violation propagates; never convert it to a typed conflict. Unique indexes arbitrate races; the reread is not an application precheck.
 
 | Existing dispatch-shaped NOT NULL field | `cold_chain` value in `0022`; `ev_charging` uses the same rule in `0025` | Constraint |

-For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. The ledger INSERT uses the event-identity partial conflict target; a unique violation on `accepted_ev_start_unique` rolls back that record's savepoint, rereads the first START, and emits `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT` in the same batch transaction. A unique violation on another constraint is not converted into this code. The session unique index is the race arbiter, not an application precheck.
+For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. Every accepted-evidence INSERT, including EVENT and EV START, retains `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT accepted_evidence_message_unique DO NOTHING`. For each ordinal, the ledger opens a savepoint around that INSERT. On a message-target `DO NOTHING` or a unique violation of `accepted_event_identity_unique` or `accepted_ev_start_unique`, it rolls back to the savepoint where needed and rereads under the same batch transaction in fixed order: message key, then event identity, then EV START identity. A matching message SHA returns `DUPLICATE`; a changed message SHA returns `MESSAGE_CONFLICT` even when the event or START key also collides. If the message key is free, a matching event SHA returns `DUPLICATE` and a changed event SHA returns `EVENT_ID_CONFLICT`; only if neither key matches does a distinct START collision return `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT`. Thus message wins a double collision, then event wins over session. Each conflict references the first accepted row for its winning key. A non-unique or unlisted unique violation propagates; never convert it to a typed conflict. Unique indexes arbitrate races; the reread is not an application precheck.

+from pathlib import Path
+
+cold = Path('.agent/W2-technical-design-cold.md')
+ev = Path('.agent/W2-technical-design-ev.md')
+dec = Path('.ai/DECISIONS.md')
+
+def line(path, prefix, replacement):
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    matches = [x for x in s.splitlines(keepends=True) if x.startswith(prefix)]
+    assert len(matches) == 1, (path, prefix, len(matches))
+    path.write_text(s.replace(matches[0], replacement + '\n'), encoding='utf-8')
+
+def sub(path, old, new):
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    assert old in s, (path, old)
+    path.write_text(s.replace(old, new), encoding='utf-8')
+
+line(cold, '| `0023_w2_cold_money`', '| `0023_w2_cold_money` (W2-04) | Reuse assumption lineage; add `cold_consequence_revision` and `cold_consequence_evidence` as described below. Add append-only `cold_consequence_work` (`id uuid PK`, tenant/site, window_start/end, domain CHECK `=\'cold_chain\'`, dirty_basis_sha256 char(64), created_at; UNIQUE `(site_id,window_start,window_end,dirty_basis_sha256)`) and `cold_consequence_work_attempt` (`id uuid PK`, tenant/site, work_id scoped FK, attempt_no int>0, outcome `SUCCEEDED|FAILED`, error_code nullable, started_at, finished_at; UNIQUE `(work_id,attempt_no)`, partial unique successful attempt per work). The revision carries `finding_revision_id`, `episode_revision_id`, positive `revision_no`, `basis_sha256`, kind `PRODUCT_EXPOSURE|RECORDED_REPAIR`, `quantity_unit` `degC_min|currency_record`, nullable ordered quantity/amount bounds, nullable currency, state `UNPRICED|WITHHELD|RECORDED`, reason, overlap_group, source_gate, recorded_at; amount/currency null unless RECORDED, PRODUCT_EXPOSURE never RECORDED in W2, scoped FKs, UNIQUE `(finding_revision_id,kind,basis_sha256)`. Evidence link has scoped consequence/evidence FKs and pair PK. Append-only triggers cover facts, revisions, links, work and attempts. New cold assumption keys have no defaults. | Refuse with cold consequence/work/attempt rows; drop new tables only, leave shared assumption history untouched. |')
+line(cold, 'All six `config/scenarios/cold-', 'All six `config/scenarios/cold-*.yaml` use `cold-scenario/1`, UTC run 2026-10-06 19:00–20:00 with a closing-boundary sample at 20:00, seed 20417, one standalone `cold-thermal/1` pack, and no mini-grid composition. The selected evidence/assessment window is half-open `[19:00,20:05)`, containing exactly 13 scheduled five-minute points from 19:00 through 20:00; no 20:05 point is included. Assets are `room-1`, `probe-1`, `door-1`, `generator-1`, `grid-1`, `bus-1`, `compressor-1`; generator backup enabled. Base initial state is T=3.00°C, ambient=20°C, door CLOSED, compressor OFF, grid/bus POWERED, generator STOPPED, transfer GRID, probe OK; excursion-derived cases override initial T to 3.80°C. Each row lists the entire non-default forcing, ordered by `(time,kind,asset)`; observation faults occur after a valid world transition and cannot bypass `PhysicallyUnacceptable` guards.')
+sub(cold, 'Public assertion in 19:00–20:00', 'Public assertion in `[19:00,20:05)`')
+sub(cold, '`cold-observation/1` freezes the three specified hold/drop/fault variants', '`simulator/assetops_simulator/observation/transform.py` owns `cold-observation/1` hold/drop/fault transform variants')
+line(cold, 'Rule 8 in `tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1`', 'Rule 8 in `tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1`: add `cold_episode,cold_episode_revision,cold_episode_evidence` to `$assessTables` before `$assessInserts` is derived; add `cold_consequence_revision,cold_consequence_evidence,cold_consequence_work,cold_consequence_work_attempt` to `$consequenceTables`. `postgres_assessment_store.py` alone inserts episode and analytic/assessment rows; `postgres_finding_store.py` alone inserts Findings. `assessment_worker.py` owns one caller-supplied transaction spanning both adapters and job completion. `postgres_consequence_store.py` alone inserts consequence rows and append-only work attempts; migration triggers alone insert work. The consequence writer leases with an advisory transaction lock per work ID, appends one `SUCCEEDED` attempt atomically with consequence/links or a `FAILED` attempt with error code, and never updates/deletes work or attempts. Cross-writer checks protect all four tables. The ledger alone inserts event evidence, rejection and disposition in one transaction. No `DO UPDATE` permission is added.')

-For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. The ledger INSERT uses the event-identity partial conflict target; a unique violation on `accepted_ev_start_unique` rolls back that record's savepoint, rereads the first START, and emits `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT` in the same batch transaction. A unique violation on another constraint is not converted into this code. The session unique index is the race arbiter, not an application precheck.
+For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. Every accepted-evidence INSERT, including EVENT and EV START, retains `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT accepted_evidence_message_unique DO NOTHING`. For each ordinal, the ledger opens a savepoint around that INSERT. On a message-target `DO NOTHING` or a unique violation of `accepted_event_identity_unique` or `accepted_ev_start_unique`, it rolls back to the savepoint where needed and rereads under the same batch transaction in fixed order: message key, then event identity, then EV START identity. A matching message SHA returns `DUPLICATE`; a changed message SHA returns `MESSAGE_CONFLICT` even when the event or START key also collides. If the message key is free, a matching event SHA returns `DUPLICATE` and a changed event SHA returns `EVENT_ID_CONFLICT`; only if neither key matches does a distinct START collision return `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT`. Thus message wins a double collision, then event wins over session. Each conflict references the first accepted row for its winning key. A non-unique or unlisted unique violation propagates; never convert it to a typed conflict. Unique indexes arbitrate races; the reread is not an application precheck.

+from pathlib import Path
+
+cold = Path('.agent/W2-technical-design-cold.md')
+ev = Path('.agent/W2-technical-design-ev.md')
+dec = Path('.ai/DECISIONS.md')
+
+def line(path, prefix, replacement):
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    matches = [x for x in s.splitlines(keepends=True) if x.startswith(prefix)]
+    assert len(matches) == 1, (path, prefix, len(matches))
+    path.write_text(s.replace(matches[0], replacement + '\n'), encoding='utf-8')
+
+def sub(path, old, new):
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    assert old in s, (path, old)
+    path.write_text(s.replace(old, new), encoding='utf-8')
+

-For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. The ledger INSERT uses the event-identity partial conflict target; a unique violation on `accepted_ev_start_unique` rolls back that record's savepoint, rereads the first START, and emits `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT` in the same batch transaction. A unique violation on another constraint is not converted into this code. The session unique index is the race arbiter, not an application precheck.
+For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. Every accepted-evidence INSERT, including EVENT and EV START, retains `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT accepted_evidence_message_unique DO NOTHING`. For each ordinal, the ledger opens a savepoint around that INSERT. On a message-target `DO NOTHING` or a unique violation of `accepted_event_identity_unique` or `accepted_ev_start_unique`, it rolls back to the savepoint where needed and rereads under the same batch transaction in fixed order: message key, then event identity, then EV START identity. A matching message SHA returns `DUPLICATE`; a changed message SHA returns `MESSAGE_CONFLICT` even when the event or START key also collides. If the message key is free, a matching event SHA returns `DUPLICATE` and a changed event SHA returns `EVENT_ID_CONFLICT`; only if neither key matches does a distinct START collision return `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT`. Thus message wins a double collision, then event wins over session. Each conflict references the first accepted row for its winning key. A non-unique or unlisted unique violation propagates; never convert it to a typed conflict. Unique indexes arbitrate races; the reread is not an application precheck.

+def line(path, prefix, replacement):
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    matches = [x for x in s.splitlines(keepends=True) if x.startswith(prefix)]
+    assert len(matches) == 1, (path, prefix, len(matches))
+    path.write_text(s.replace(matches[0], replacement + '\n'), encoding='utf-8')
+
+def sub(path, old, new):
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    assert old in s, (path, old)
+    path.write_text(s.replace(old, new), encoding='utf-8')

-For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. The ledger INSERT uses the event-identity partial conflict target; a unique violation on `accepted_ev_start_unique` rolls back that record's savepoint, rereads the first START, and emits `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT` in the same batch transaction. A unique violation on another constraint is not converted into this code. The session unique index is the race arbiter, not an application precheck.
+For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. Every accepted-evidence INSERT, including EVENT and EV START, retains `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT accepted_evidence_message_unique DO NOTHING`. For each ordinal, the ledger opens a savepoint around that INSERT. On a message-target `DO NOTHING` or a unique violation of `accepted_event_identity_unique` or `accepted_ev_start_unique`, it rolls back to the savepoint where needed and rereads under the same batch transaction in fixed order: message key, then event identity, then EV START identity. A matching message SHA returns `DUPLICATE`; a changed message SHA returns `MESSAGE_CONFLICT` even when the event or START key also collides. If the message key is free, a matching event SHA returns `DUPLICATE` and a changed event SHA returns `EVENT_ID_CONFLICT`; only if neither key matches does a distinct START collision return `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT`. Thus message wins a double collision, then event wins over session. Each conflict references the first accepted row for its winning key. A non-unique or unlisted unique violation propagates; never convert it to a typed conflict. Unique indexes arbitrate races; the reread is not an application precheck.

+def line(path, prefix, replacement):
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    matches = [x for x in s.splitlines(keepends=True) if x.startswith(prefix)]
+    assert len(matches) == 1, (path, prefix, len(matches))
+    path.write_text(s.replace(matches[0], replacement + '\n'), encoding='utf-8')
+
+def sub(path, old, new):
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    assert old in s, (path, old)
+    path.write_text(s.replace(old, new), encoding='utf-8')

-For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. The ledger INSERT uses the event-identity partial conflict target; a unique violation on `accepted_ev_start_unique` rolls back that record's savepoint, rereads the first START, and emits `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT` in the same batch transaction. A unique violation on another constraint is not converted into this code. The session unique index is the race arbiter, not an application precheck.
+For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. Every accepted-evidence INSERT, including EVENT and EV START, retains `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT accepted_evidence_message_unique DO NOTHING`. For each ordinal, the ledger opens a savepoint around that INSERT. On a message-target `DO NOTHING` or a unique violation of `accepted_event_identity_unique` or `accepted_ev_start_unique`, it rolls back to the savepoint where needed and rereads under the same batch transaction in fixed order: message key, then event identity, then EV START identity. A matching message SHA returns `DUPLICATE`; a changed message SHA returns `MESSAGE_CONFLICT` even when the event or START key also collides. If the message key is free, a matching event SHA returns `DUPLICATE` and a changed event SHA returns `EVENT_ID_CONFLICT`; only if neither key matches does a distinct START collision return `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT`. Thus message wins a double collision, then event wins over session. Each conflict references the first accepted row for its winning key. A non-unique or unlisted unique violation propagates; never convert it to a typed conflict. Unique indexes arbitrate races; the reread is not an application precheck.

+def line(path, prefix, replacement):
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    matches = [x for x in s.splitlines(keepends=True) if x.startswith(prefix)]
+    assert len(matches) == 1, (path, prefix, len(matches))
+    path.write_text(s.replace(matches[0], replacement + '\n'), encoding='utf-8')
+
+def sub(path, old, new):
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    assert old in s, (path, old)
+    path.write_text(s.replace(old, new), encoding='utf-8')
+

-For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. The ledger INSERT uses the event-identity partial conflict target; a unique violation on `accepted_ev_start_unique` rolls back that record's savepoint, rereads the first START, and emits `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT` in the same batch transaction. A unique violation on another constraint is not converted into this code. The session unique index is the race arbiter, not an application precheck.
+For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. Every accepted-evidence INSERT, including EVENT and EV START, retains `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT accepted_evidence_message_unique DO NOTHING`. For each ordinal, the ledger opens a savepoint around that INSERT. On a message-target `DO NOTHING` or a unique violation of `accepted_event_identity_unique` or `accepted_ev_start_unique`, it rolls back to the savepoint where needed and rereads under the same batch transaction in fixed order: message key, then event identity, then EV START identity. A matching message SHA returns `DUPLICATE`; a changed message SHA returns `MESSAGE_CONFLICT` even when the event or START key also collides. If the message key is free, a matching event SHA returns `DUPLICATE` and a changed event SHA returns `EVENT_ID_CONFLICT`; only if neither key matches does a distinct START collision return `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT`. Thus message wins a double collision, then event wins over session. Each conflict references the first accepted row for its winning key. A non-unique or unlisted unique violation propagates; never convert it to a typed conflict. Unique indexes arbitrate races; the reread is not an application precheck.

+from pathlib import Path
+
+cold = Path('.agent/W2-technical-design-cold.md')
+ev = Path('.agent/W2-technical-design-ev.md')
+dec = Path('.ai/DECISIONS.md')
+
+def line(path, prefix, replacement):
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    matches = [x for x in s.splitlines(keepends=True) if x.startswith(prefix)]
+    assert len(matches) == 1, (path, prefix, len(matches))
+    path.write_text(s.replace(matches[0], replacement + '\n'), encoding='utf-8')
+
+def sub(path, old, new):
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    assert old in s, (path, old)
+    path.write_text(s.replace(old, new), encoding='utf-8')
+

+For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. Every accepted-evidence INSERT, including EVENT and EV START, retains `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT accepted_evidence_message_unique DO NOTHING`. For each ordinal, the ledger opens a savepoint around that INSERT. On a message-target `DO NOTHING` or a unique violation of `accepted_event_identity_unique` or `accepted_ev_start_unique`, it rolls back to the savepoint where needed and rereads under the same batch transaction in fixed order: message key, then event identity, then EV START identity. A matching message SHA returns `DUPLICATE`; a changed message SHA returns `MESSAGE_CONFLICT` even when the event or START key also collides. If the message key is free, a matching event SHA returns `DUPLICATE` and a changed event SHA returns `EVENT_ID_CONFLICT`; only if neither key matches does a distinct START collision return `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT`. Thus message wins a double collision, then event wins over session. Each conflict references the first accepted row for its winning key. A non-unique or unlisted unique violation propagates; never convert it to a typed conflict. Unique indexes arbitrate races; the reread is not an application precheck.

+def line(path, prefix, replacement):
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    matches = [x for x in s.splitlines(keepends=True) if x.startswith(prefix)]
+    assert len(matches) == 1, (path, prefix, len(matches))
+    path.write_text(s.replace(matches[0], replacement + '\n'), encoding='utf-8')
+
+def sub(path, old, new):
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    assert old in s, (path, old)
+    path.write_text(s.replace(old, new), encoding='utf-8')

 `0022` adds nullable `finding.subject_asset_id uuid` with scoped asset FK; relaxes `finding.generator_asset_id` to nullable; adds CHECK `(domain='mini_grid' AND generator_asset_id IS NOT NULL) OR (domain='cold_chain' AND subject_asset_id IS NOT NULL AND generator_asset_id IS NULL)`. It also adds `finding_revision.domain text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'mini_grid'` for existing rows, drops the default after backfill, adds UNIQUE `(tenant_id,site_id,id,domain)` on `finding`, and replaces the revision's parent FK with `(tenant_id,site_id,finding_id,domain)` to that unique key. Thus revision CHECKs can use their own stored domain without trusting a caller. `0025` widens both domain CHECKs with `'ev_charging'`. One literal per domain is used in Finding, FindingRevision, `recompute_job.domain`, and episode/session producers: `mini_grid`, `cold_chain`, `ev_charging`. Existing `mini_grid` rows stay untouched apart from the additive revision domain backfill. `recompute_job.domain` has no SQL CHECK. `0022` adds the `(tenant_id,site_id,domain,subject_asset_id,anchor_start)` index and updates Finding store/read selection by domain. The shared fuel consequence CHECK remains intact. The `0020` accepted-event unique index is enforced in the ledger transaction; race losers get the conflict disposition above.
 
-For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. The ledger INSERT uses the event-identity partial conflict target; a unique violation on `accepted_ev_start_unique` rolls back that record's savepoint, rereads the first START, and emits `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT` in the same batch transaction. A unique violation on another constraint is not converted into this code. The session unique index is the race arbiter, not an application precheck.
+For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. Every accepted-evidence INSERT, including EVENT and EV START, retains `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT accepted_evidence_message_unique DO NOTHING`. For each ordinal, the ledger opens a savepoint around that INSERT. On a message-target `DO NOTHING` or a unique violation of `accepted_event_identity_unique` or `accepted_ev_start_unique`, it rolls back to the savepoint where needed and rereads under the same batch transaction in fixed order: message key, then event identity, then EV START identity. A matching message SHA returns `DUPLICATE`; a changed message SHA returns `MESSAGE_CONFLICT` even when the event or START key also collides. If the message key is free, a matching event SHA returns `DUPLICATE` and a changed event SHA returns `EVENT_ID_CONFLICT`; only if neither key matches does a distinct START collision return `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT`. Thus message wins a double collision, then event wins over session. Each conflict references the first accepted row for its winning key. A non-unique or unlisted unique violation propagates; never convert it to a typed conflict. Unique indexes arbitrate races; the reread is not an application precheck.

+from pathlib import Path
+
+cold = Path('.agent/W2-technical-design-cold.md')

+def line(path, prefix, replacement):
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    matches = [x for x in s.splitlines(keepends=True) if x.startswith(prefix)]
+    assert len(matches) == 1, (path, prefix, len(matches))
+    path.write_text(s.replace(matches[0], replacement + '\n'), encoding='utf-8')
+
+def sub(path, old, new):
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    assert old in s, (path, old)
+    path.write_text(s.replace(old, new), encoding='utf-8')
+

-For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. The ledger INSERT uses the event-identity partial conflict target; a unique violation on `accepted_ev_start_unique` rolls back that record's savepoint, rereads the first START, and emits `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT` in the same batch transaction. +For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. Every accepted-evidence INSERT, including EVENT and EV START, retains `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT accepted_evidence_message_unique DO NOTHING`. For each ordinal, the ledger opens a savepoint around that INSERT. On a message-target `DO NOTHING` or a unique violation of `accepted_event_identity_unique` or `accepted_ev_start_unique`, it rolls back to the savepoint where needed and rereads under the same batch transaction in fixed order: message key, then event identity, then EV START identity. A matching message SHA returns `DUPLICATE`; a changed message SHA returns `MESSAGE_CONFLICT` even when the event or START key also collides. If the message key is free, a matching event SHA returns `DUPLICATE` and a changed event SHA returns `EVENT_ID_CONFLICT`; only if neither key matches does a distinct START collision return `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT`. Thus message wins a double collision, then event wins over session. Each conflict references the first accepted row for its winning key. A non-unique or unlisted unique violation propagates; never convert it to a typed conflict. Unique indexes arbitrate races; the reread is not an application precheck.

 Grid loss opens transfer and deenergizes compressor bus; controller emits `START_REQUEST` only if backup enabled, generator reaches RUNNING after delay unless failed, transfer closes to BACKUP after delay if generator power exists, and compressor restarts after its own delay if controller still demands cooling. Failed start emits `START_RESULT/FAILED` at timeout; successful start emits `START_RESULT/SUCCEEDED` when running. Transfer result is emitted only if a position sensor is configured; absence stays absence. Door events are sensor transitions; door opening adds heat only while open. Probe failure emits one `UNAVAILABLE` reading with reason `SENSOR_FAILURE` if the profile publishes it, then no numeric sample; reporting-path loss emits no envelope. Device measurement is `T + fixed observation bias` (default 0), rounded to 0.1°C by half-even rule; no private cause field. Controller and power events use observed public transitions and stable correlation ID.

+For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. Every accepted-evidence INSERT, including EVENT and EV START, retains `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT accepted_evidence_message_unique DO NOTHING`. For each ordinal, the ledger opens a savepoint around that INSERT. On a message-target `DO NOTHING` or a unique violation of `accepted_event_identity_unique` or `accepted_ev_start_unique`, it rolls back to the savepoint where needed and rereads under the same batch transaction in fixed order: message key, then event identity, then EV START identity. A matching message SHA returns `DUPLICATE`; a changed message SHA returns `MESSAGE_CONFLICT` even when the event or START key also collides. If the message key is free, a matching event SHA returns `DUPLICATE` and a changed event SHA returns `EVENT_ID_CONFLICT`; only if neither key matches does a distinct START collision return `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT`. Thus message wins a double collision, then event wins over session. Each conflict references the first accepted row for its winning key. A non-unique or unlisted unique violation propagates; never convert it to a typed conflict. Unique indexes arbitrate races; the reread is not an application precheck.

-For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. The ledger INSERT uses the event-identity partial conflict target; a unique violation on `accepted_ev_start_unique` rolls back that record's savepoint, rereads the first START, and emits `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT` in the same batch transaction. +For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. Every accepted-evidence INSERT, including EVENT and EV START, retains `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT accepted_evidence_message_unique DO NOTHING`. For each ordinal, the ledger opens a savepoint around that INSERT. On a message-target `DO NOTHING` or a unique violation of `accepted_event_identity_unique` or `accepted_ev_start_unique`, it rolls back to the savepoint where needed and rereads under the same batch transaction in fixed order: message key, then event identity, then EV START identity. A matching message SHA returns `DUPLICATE`; a changed message SHA returns `MESSAGE_CONFLICT` even when the event or START key also collides. If the message key is free, a matching event SHA returns `DUPLICATE` and a changed event SHA returns `EVENT_ID_CONFLICT`; only if neither key matches does a distinct START collision return `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT`. Thus message wins a double collision, then event wins over session. Each conflict references the first accepted row for its winning key. A non-unique or unlisted unique violation propagates; never convert it to a typed conflict. Unique indexes arbitrate races; the reread is not an application precheck.

.agent/W2-technical-design-cold.md:138:Cold money work is triggered by appended cold FindingRevision or episode revision, relevant assumption publication or source-gate change; a migration trigger inserts append-only dirty `(site,window,domain='cold_chain')` work with unique dirty key and `DO NOTHING` duplicate suppression. `financials/recompute.py` runs the consequence worker; it selects unfinished work lacking a successful attempt, locks per work ID, selects the exact settled assessment/Finding basis, writes immutable consequence/evidence rows through `postgres_consequence_store.py`, and appends a `SUCCEEDED` attempt in the same transaction. Failure appends a `FAILED` attempt with an error code, leaving work retryable; no work status UPDATE exists. `tools/writer_guard_probes.py` gains pass probes for each owner/table, message-target conflict and attempt append, and fail probes for runtime work INSERT, foreign writer, immutable UPDATE/DELETE, `DO UPDATE`, and event partial-index conflict target. Include double-collision probes: message+event must choose message; event+session with a free message must choose event. `configuration-persistence.ps1` counts probes and fails closed when an owner is absent. `overlap_group` is SHA-256 of canonical `(tenant_id,site_id,window_start,window_end,room_asset_id)` prefixed `cold:`; excursion and backup consequences for that room/window share it.

.agent/W2-technical-design-ev.md:105:Rule 8 `tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1`: add `ev_session,ev_session_revision,ev_session_evidence,ev_meter_interval` to `$assessTables` before `$assessInserts` is derived; add `ev_consequence_revision,ev_consequence_session,ev_consequence_evidence` to `$consequenceTables`, then add `ev_consequence_work,ev_consequence_work_attempt` to the same `$consequenceTables` array. `postgres_assessment_store.py` exclusively inserts the four session tables, `postgres_finding_store.py` exclusively inserts Findings, and `postgres_consequence_store.py` exclusively inserts the three EV consequence tables and appends work attempts. Migration triggers alone insert work; the consequence writer appends a `SUCCEEDED` attempt atomically with consequence/links or a `FAILED` attempt with error code. Work and attempts are append-only; runtime work INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE are rejected. Cross-writer checks cover `$consequenceTables`. `assessment_worker.py` owns one caller-supplied transaction containing analytic run, assessment, session revision/links, FindingRevision/links and job completion. `financials/recompute.py` owns a separate transaction containing consequence/links and successful work-attempt append. No SQL writer enters HTTP/domain modules and no `DO UPDATE` allowlist entry is added. The 0020 message-only conflict target, per-ordinal savepoint/reread precedence and Receipt V2 classification are inherited unchanged. `tools/writer_guard_probes.py` adds permitted owner INSERT and work-attempt append, rejects runtime work INSERT, immutable work/attempt UPDATE or DELETE, foreign owner, immutable UPDATE/DELETE, unlisted `ON CONFLICT` and dynamic SQL; it includes pass/fail probes for both multi-adapter transaction paths. The guard fails when a named module/table is missing.

 | `contradictory` | Same world forcing/initial state as `excursion`; observation fault `(19:05,START_RESULT,generator-1,SUCCEEDED)` emits an additional distinct public controller event ID beside the genuine FAILED event, both GOOD and same correlation ID; door CLOSED. | Both events are individually valid and accepted; start-response claim is `CONTRADICTORY`, never `OBSERVED_FAILURE`. The frozen observation profile, not the physical model, creates the conflicting report. |

 `0022` adds nullable `finding.subject_asset_id uuid` with scoped asset FK; relaxes `finding.generator_asset_id` to nullable; adds CHECK `(domain='mini_grid' AND generator_asset_id IS NOT NULL) OR (domain='cold_chain' AND subject_asset_id IS NOT NULL AND generator_asset_id IS NULL)`. It also adds `finding_revision.domain text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'mini_grid'` for existing rows, drops the default after backfill, adds UNIQUE `(tenant_id,site_id,id,domain)` on `finding`, and replaces the revision's parent FK with `(tenant_id,site_id,finding_id,domain)` to that unique key. Thus revision CHECKs can use their own stored domain without trusting a caller. `0025` widens both domain CHECKs with `'ev_charging'`. One literal per domain is used in Finding, FindingRevision, `recompute_job.domain`, and episode/session producers: `mini_grid`, `cold_chain`, `ev_charging`. Existing `mini_grid` rows stay untouched apart from the additive revision domain backfill. `recompute_job.domain` has no SQL CHECK. `0022` adds the `(tenant_id,site_id,domain,subject_asset_id,anchor_start)` index and updates Finding store/read selection by domain. The shared fuel consequence CHECK remains intact. The `0020` accepted-event unique index is enforced in the ledger transaction; race losers get the conflict disposition above.
 
-For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. The ledger INSERT uses the event-identity partial conflict target; a unique violation on `accepted_ev_start_unique` rolls back that record's savepoint, rereads the first START, and emits `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT` in the same batch transaction. A unique violation on another constraint is not converted into this code. The session unique index is the race arbiter, not an application precheck.
+For EV session events, `accepted_evidence.asset_id` is the effective bound EV_CONNECTOR UUID and `connector_id` is its canonical public identifier; S2 resolves the equality before INSERT. Every accepted-evidence INSERT, including EVENT and EV START, retains `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT accepted_evidence_message_unique DO NOTHING`. For each ordinal, the ledger opens a savepoint around that INSERT. On a message-target `DO NOTHING` or a unique violation of `accepted_event_identity_unique` or `accepted_ev_start_unique`, it rolls back to the savepoint where needed and rereads under the same batch transaction in fixed order: message key, then event identity, then EV START identity. A matching message SHA returns `DUPLICATE`; a changed message SHA returns `MESSAGE_CONFLICT` even when the event or START key also collides. If the message key is free, a matching event SHA returns `DUPLICATE` and a changed event SHA returns `EVENT_ID_CONFLICT`; only if neither key matches does a distinct START collision return `SESSION_ID_CONFLICT`. Thus message wins a double collision, then event wins over session. Each conflict references the first accepted row for its winning key. A non-unique or unlisted unique violation propagates; never convert it to a typed conflict. Unique indexes arbitrate races; the reread is not an application precheck.
